The AP model would preserve that ownership while isolating it from transport/device technology."}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"5.2 Capabilities (ports)"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"In AP terms, Capabilities are domain-owned abstractions for external needs."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"In OTA, many "},{"type":"text","text":"HalOtaGeneric","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" base classes behave this way (time, filesystem, card readers, pinpad, etc.): they are interfaces the business behavior depends on, without hardcoding one device/platform implementation."}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"5.3 Adapters"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"In AP terms, adapters are concrete technology bindings."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"In OTA, "},{"type":"text","text":"HalOtaValina","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" and "},{"type":"text","text":"HalOtaCTOS","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" provide exactly that: concrete platform/device implementations behind the abstract capability interfaces."}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"5.4 Application / Host boundary"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"In AP terms, "},{"type":"text","text":"Application","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" orchestrates use cases, while host-side incoming implementations handle transport/runtime concerns."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"In OTA, "},{"type":"text","text":"OtaApi","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" and event-handler integration ("},{"type":"text","text":"IOtaAPIEventHandler_T","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":") together form much of this boundary. The AP model separates these concerns more explicitly, but the intent is already familiar."}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"6. Shared Kernel in OTA"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The AP model defines a dedicated "},{"type":"text","text":"SharedKernel","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" for stable, reusable business datatypes."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"In OTA, equivalent datatypes are present but distributed (for example message and amount-related types in "},{"type":"text","text":"OtaApi","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":"/"},{"type":"text","text":"Ota","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" headers). A key AP improvement is to gather shared business datatypes into an explicit, disciplined shared-kernel area."}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"7. Practical onboarding checklist for OTA engineers"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"When moving from OTA mental model to AP model:"}]},{"type":"orderedList","attrs":{"order":1},"content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Identify business decision logic first ("},{"type":"text","text":"Domain","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":")."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Identify abstract external needs next ("},{"type":"text","text":"Capabilities","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" / ports)."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Move platform specifics behind those interfaces ("},{"type":"text","text":"Adapters","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":")."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Keep incoming transport/runtime code in host boundaries ("},{"type":"text","text":"Host Units","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":")."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Keep shared business datatypes explicit and stable ("},{"type":"text","text":"SharedKernel","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":")."}]}]}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"8. Relationship to normative AP documents"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Use this introduction as a vocabulary bridge only. Normative requirements are in:"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"AP-001 (Architectural Principles)"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"AP-002 (Service Structure)"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"AP-003 (Incoming Implementations)"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"AP-004 (Domain)"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"AP-005 (Capabilities)"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"AP-006 (Dependency Rules)"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"AP-007 (Adapters)"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"AP-008 (Testing)"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"AP-009 (Read Models and Queries)"}]}]}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"If this introduction and any AP conflict, the AP document governs."}]}]}

Introduction"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"This AP expands the Domain principles of AP-001 (§6.3) and the Domain Unit structure of AP-002 (§5.1) into implementation guidance."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"It defines how business rules, entities, policies, services, and outcomes are organized within the Domain Unit, and where the Domain's Capability abstractions and shared business datatypes (the Shared Kernel) reside."}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"2. Normative Language"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The key words "},{"type":"text","text":"MUST","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":", "},{"type":"text","text":"MUST NOT","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":", "},{"type":"text","text":"SHALL","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":", "},{"type":"text","text":"SHOULD","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":", and "},{"type":"text","text":"MAY","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" in this document are to be interpreted as described in BCP 14 (RFC 2119 [1] and RFC 8174 [2]) when, and only when, they appear in all capitals, as shown here."}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"3. Terminology"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The logical terms "},{"type":"text","text":"Entity","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":", "},{"type":"text","text":"Value Object","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":", "},{"type":"text","text":"Domain Service","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":", and "},{"type":"text","text":"Business Decision","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" retain the meanings defined in AP-001 [3]."}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Outcome","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" — The business result produced by a Domain operation; the expression of a Business Decision (AP-001 §6.1)."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Policy","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" — A named business rule or set of rules that governs whether and how a Business Decision is made."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Aggregate","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" — A cluster of Entities and Value Objects treated as a single consistency boundary for business operations."}]}]}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"4. Domain Contents"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The Domain Unit SHALL contain only business meaning, expressed through the following elements:"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Business rules."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Entities."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Value Objects (see §7)."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Policies."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Domain Services."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Outcomes."}]}]}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"These elements divide into two categories, following AP-001 §6.3:"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Business State","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" is modelled by Entities and Value Objects."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Business Behaviour","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" is modelled by business rules, Policies, and Domain Services."}]}]}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The Domain SHALL keep Business State and Business Behaviour distinct so that behaviour can be verified independently of the transport and persistence mechanisms that carry state."}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"5. Decision Ownership"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Every business decision SHALL be made within exactly one Domain (AP-001 §6.1)."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A Domain operation SHALL express its result as an "},{"type":"text","text":"Outcome","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" (§3): an explicit business value that names the decision reached, rather than a transport- or persistence-specific status."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"An Outcome SHALL be returned to the Application as a domain type; the Application translates it into a Contract Model response (AP-003), and Implementations translate it into transport-specific forms (AP-003, AP-007)."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"An Outcome MUST NOT carry transport, persistence, or serialization concerns (§7.3)."}]}]}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"6. Coordination"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"This AP distinguishes "},{"type":"text","text":"mechanical orchestration","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" from "},{"type":"text","text":"business coordination","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":"."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Evans [8] places Application Services as the natural coordinator of domain objects: they load aggregates, invoke domain operations in sequence, and bracket transactions. AP-004 follows that view for mechanical orchestration — call sequencing, transaction management, retries, and error handling that carry no business rule. That work belongs to the Application."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Business coordination is different. If changing, omitting, or reordering Domain operations would change the "},{"type":"text","text":"business correctness","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" of the result, the coordination itself expresses a business rule. That rule SHALL remain inside the Domain, not in the Application."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Where such a rule spans more than one Aggregate, it SHALL be expressed by a "},{"type":"text","text":"Domain Service","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" within the Domain Unit. The Application invokes a single Domain entry point per use case and receives an Outcome; it MUST NOT contain the business conditions that decide whether or how the participating Aggregates are affected."}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"6.1 Rationale"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Vernon [9] gives a more pragmatic guide to aggregate design than Evans's broader original treatment: if two parts must always change together to protect one invariant, they are usually one Aggregate and SHOULD be modelled as such. AP-004 adopts that view."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Accordingly, before introducing a Domain Service for cross-aggregate coordination, the model SHOULD first be checked to see whether one Aggregate has been split too aggressively. If the Aggregates are correctly separate, eventual consistency is preferred: one Aggregate produces an Outcome stating the business fact that occurred, and follow-up effects on other Aggregates happen asynchronously outside the originating transaction."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A Domain Service is therefore the narrow case, not the default. It is used only when the Aggregates are correctly separate, eventual consistency is not sufficient, and a business decision still depends on coordinating more than one Aggregate."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The danger of leaving that decision in the Application is that the Application would no longer be merely arranging execution; it would be deciding business correctness. That would violate the Decision Ownership invariant of AP-001 §6.1."}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"6.2 Boundaries"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A Domain Service SHALL coordinate only Domain elements (Entities, Value Objects, Policies, other Domain Services). It MUST NOT invoke the Application, a Host, or any Implementation."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A Domain Service MAY depend on Capabilities (AP-005) to obtain what the Domain requires, but the decision it reaches remains within the Domain."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Coordination that is purely mechanical (dependency injection, call sequencing, transaction bracketing, error handling carrying no business rule) is not business coordination. It belongs to the Application."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The distinguishing test is whether the coordination determines a business outcome or merely arranges execution. If it merely arranges execution, it SHALL NOT be modelled as a Domain Service."}]}]}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"6.3 Aggregate Consistency and Transactions"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Each Aggregate SHALL preserve its own invariants through its Aggregate Root."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A Domain Service that coordinates multiple Aggregates SHALL invoke their public Domain operations; it MUST NOT bypass an Aggregate Root to modify an internal Entity."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A multi-Aggregate operation SHALL state whether its business outcome requires atomic consistency or permits eventual consistency."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Where atomic consistency is required, the Application establishes the transaction boundary (AP-001 §6.2) and the relevant Capability and Adapter realize it. The Domain Service expresses the business rule that requires the coordinated change; it does not implement the transaction mechanism."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Where eventual consistency is permitted, the Domain Service's Outcome SHALL make the resulting business fact, resulting state, or required follow-up explicit. That Outcome MAY be used to publish a domain event or equivalent notification outside the Domain. Any delivery, retry, or compensation mechanics remain outside the Domain."}]}]}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"7. Value Objects and the Shared Kernel"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A Value Object is an immutable, data-carrying business type identified by its values. Structurally, a Value Object differs little from a Message (AP-003): both are shapes of data. This AP therefore treats shared Value Objects as part of the "},{"type":"text","text":"Shared Kernel","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" (AP-001 §6.4, AP-002 §5.1.2) so that a single Definition"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A shared Value Object SHALL be defined once as a data-carrying type "},{"type":"text","text":"SharedKernel/","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" folder of the Core Unit, a peer folder alongside "},{"type":"text","text":"Domain/","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":", "},{"type":"text","text":"Capabilities/","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":", and on it, dependency direction is preserved without introducing bidirectional coupling (AP-001 §6.4, AP-002 "},{"type":"text","text":"Domain/","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" folder; only shared business datatypes belong in the Shared Constraints"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The Domain MUST NOT depend on any Host or Implementation, and MUST NOT depend on the Application (AP-002 §7). The Domain MAY depend on the Shared Kernel and Capabilities (AP-001 §6.4, AP-002 §7)."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"No transport, persistence, messaging, deployment, or framework constructs (AP-001 §6.3)."}]}]}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"10. Compliance"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A Domain implementation complies with this AP the two kept distinct (§4)."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Every business decision is made within exactly one Domain and expressed as an (§7)."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Value Objects and Outcomes remain pure business types, free of transport, persistence, and serialization "},{"type":"text","text":"Capabilities/","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" folder (§8)."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The A Deployment Unit contains composition and operational startup concerns only; it is not a place for transport-specific endpoint implementation or business rules."}]}]}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"4. Host Unit Structure"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Each inbound transport or trigger mechanism SHALL be realized as a dedicated Host Unit. A Host Unit is organized to contain all the code required to receive external stimuli or runtime triggers through that mechanism and to compose a runnable service."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A distinction applies to how many wirings a single Host Unit may publish (§5.7.4):"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Transport-defined","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" Host Units are bound to an externally-defined technology (HTTP, a message broker, gRPC). Each such technology SHALL have its own dedicated Host Unit, because the technology defines the runtime, connection lifecycle, and failure model."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Internally-triggered","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" Host Units are driven by internal logic rather than an external technology (scheduling, polling). A single such Host Unit MAY publish more than one wiring for the same mechanism (for example, several schedules within one "},{"type":"text","text":"SchedulerHost","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":")."}]}]}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"5.1 Infrastructure Implementation Unit Organization"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"An Infrastructure Implementation Unit SHALL keep its internal shape minimal and technology-appropriate."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"An Infrastructure Implementation Unit MUST provide:"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Transport/trigger-specific endpoint code that receives external Models"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Configuration registration callable from Deployment composition"}]}]}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Folder names and file layout MAY vary by technology and language, provided these responsibilities remain explicit and testable."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Scheduling and polling are variants of the same internally-triggered pattern (§5): both are driven by internal logic rather than an external technology, and a single such Host Unit MAY realize either or both."}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"5.2 Infrastructure Implementation Responsibilities"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"An Infrastructure Implementation Unit is responsible for protocol translation and invocation only."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Transport "},{"type":"text","text":"Configuration/","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" folder contains the logic that exposes the Application through the host transport or trigger mechanism. This includes:"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Dependency injection container registration for the host and Application"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Middleware configuration (for HTTP hosts)"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Transport- or trigger-specific setup (consumer registration for MassTransit, command registration for CLI, schedule registration, polling intervals, saga runtime configuration)"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Configuration loading and validation"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Host-level monitoring setup such as health checks"}]}]}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Configuration ensures that Transport Endpoints receive the Application services they need while remaining isolated from business logic and transport-independent concerns."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Configuration MAY invoke infrastructure initialization at startup — for example, triggering a Persistence Adapter's schema-readiness step so that migration completes before the host serves traffic. Such initialization is an "},{"type":"text","text":"Adapter concern owned by AP-007 §8","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":": Configuration invokes it but MUST NOT contain the migration logic itself, which resides in the Adapter (AP-007 §8.1, §8.3)."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Configuration logic MUST NOT contain business rules. It is purely mechanical wiring."}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"5.4 Dependency Flow"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The Host     ↓                 ↓                 ↓\n    (in Host)         (in Host)         (in Core)\n                                            ↓\n                                       Domain\n                                     (in Core)"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Transport (AP-007)."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A Composition Unit MAY expose host-level operational concerns (such as health checks) as "},{"type":"text","text":"Configuration/","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" SHALL provide a registration routine that a Composition Unit can §5.1."}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A "},{"type":"text","text":"transport-defined","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" Host Unit (HTTP, message broker, gRPC) SHALL remain bound to its single external technology. A Host Unit that publishes both schedules and polls, or that mixes an external transport with an internal trigger, is a monolithic host of the kind retired by AP-002 §6.2; such combinations SHALL instead be composed through a Composition Unit (§5.7.1)."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Each wiring MUST remain independently registrable and Commerce.AppHost/"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"In this structure, each Infrastructure Implementation Unit remains bound to exactly one Core, while Deployment Units may compose units from one or many Cores."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Service folders under "},{"type":"text","text":"src/","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" (for example "},{"type":"text","text":"Payment.Service/","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":", "},{"type":"text","text":"Loyalty.Service/","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":") commonly contain units intended for package publication (for example NuGet), while top-level deployment projects contain local composition/runtime entry points."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"This separation is organizational: it preserves Infrastructure Implementation Unit purity and discoverability, while leaving deployment topology and runtime packaging decisions to implementation."}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":3},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"5.7.3 Health and Liveness Signals"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A host-level liveness, readiness, or health signal (for example, an HTTP probe indicating the process is running) is an "},{"type":"text","text":"operational concern","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":", not an Incoming Implementation."}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Such a signal SHALL be configured as host-level Configuration (§5.3, §7.3) and MUST NOT invoke a Use Case or reach the Domain (§9)."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A Composition Unit MAY expose such a signal even when it composes only non-HTTP Host Units."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A signal that changes business state (for example, recording that an external device was last seen) is "},{"type":"text","text":"not","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" a health signal; it is an Incoming Implementation and SHALL be realized as a Transport Endpoint invoking a write Use Case (§6.4), with the presence rule owned by the Domain (AP-001 §6.1)."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"For .NET services, the default implementation SHOULD use the standardized observability composition ("},{"type":"text","text":"app.UseLogosObservability()","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":") rather than service-local custom probe endpoint logic."}]}]}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"6. Messages and Use Cases"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"(Outline — to be specified.)","marks":[{"type":"em"}]}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"An incoming Message typically corresponds to exactly one "},{"type":"text","text":"Use Case","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":". A Use Case is defined by a service that handles a single incoming Message."}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"6.1 Naming"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A Use Case service SHALL be named after the Message it handles, with the suffix "},{"type":"text","text":"UseCase","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" appended. For example, a "},{"type":"text","text":"Quote","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" Message is handled by a "},{"type":"text","text":"QuoteUseCase","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" service."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The naming SHALL follow the service's casing convention (AP-002 §6.1)."}]}]}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"6.2 Concrete Implementation"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A Use Case service SHALL be implemented as a concrete type with concrete methods. It MUST NOT be derived from, or hidden behind, an abstraction (interface) introduced solely to enable substitution."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Use Case services are "},{"type":"text","text":"not","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" intended to be mocked. Tests SHALL exercise the concrete Use Case service; substitution belongs at the Capability boundary (AP-005), not at the Use Case."}]}]}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"6.3 Placement"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The Use Case service and its Messages (both incoming and response) SHALL reside in the "},{"type":"text","text":"Contracts","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" Module of the Core Unit, within the "},{"type":"text","text":"Application/Contracts/","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" folder."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Use Cases are part of the Core, not part of any Host Unit. Host Units reference the Core and invoke Use Cases through their public API."}]}]}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"6.4 Read/Write Split"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"(Outline — to be specified.)","marks":[{"type":"em"}]}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Contracts SHOULD normally be split into "},{"type":"text","text":"read","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" and "},{"type":"text","text":"write","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" contracts, following a CQRS-style separation:"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Read (query) contracts","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" — Messages that retrieve information without changing business state. Their Use Case services return data and produce no business outcome."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Write (command) contracts","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" — Messages that request a change to business state. Their Use Case services delegate the decision to the Domain (AP-001 §6.1) and return the outcome."}]}]}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"This split is the usual, recommended organization. A service MAY keep contracts unsplit where the distinction adds no value, but SHOULD document the deviation."}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"6.5 Documentation"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Documentation comments SHALL be placed with the "},{"type":"text","text":"Message definition","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" so that the Message remains the single documented contract. This applies to both incoming Messages and response Messages."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Comments SHALL be formatted as required by the tool that generates the service's documentation — for example Swashbuckle (OpenAPI) on .NET, or Doxygen on C++."}]}]}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"7. Responsibilities"}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"7.1 Transport Endpoint Responsibilities"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Transport Endpoints are responsible for:"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Receiving external payloads or envelopes"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Receiving time-based or runtime triggers where applicable"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Invoking Mappings and Application Use Cases"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Enforcing transport-level concerns (HTTP status codes, message acknowledgment, protocol framing)"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Handling transport-specific errors (connection failures, timeouts, malformed requests)"}]}]}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Transport Endpoints MUST NOT:"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Contain business logic or make business decisions"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Bypass the Application layer"}]}]}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"7.2 Mapping Responsibilities"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Mappings are responsible for:"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Translating external payloads to Application Contract Models (Messages)"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Translating Application responses back to transport-specific formats"}]}]}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Mappings MUST NOT:"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Contain business logic"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Determine business outcomes"}]}]}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"7.3 Configuration Responsibilities"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Configuration logic is responsible for:"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Configuring the dependency injection container"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Loading and validating connections, schedulers, pollers, saga runtime)"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Registering host-level health checks and monitoring hooks"}]}]}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Configuration MUST NOT:"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Contain business logic"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Contain conditional logic based on business rules"}]}]}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"8. this AP."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Incoming DTOs MAY be choice MUST NOT affect:"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The design of the service may be used"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Other languages","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":": Follow language-specific platform."}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"9. Constraints"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Host Units and their adapters are subject to the following constraints:"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Each Host Unit MUST depend on exactly one Core Unit; the Core MUST NOT depend on any Host Unit (AP-002 §7)."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Transport Endpoints MUST depend only on the Application layer of the Core (§5.2)."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Transport Endpoints MUST NOT contain business rules or state."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Transport Endpoints MUST NOT determine business outcomes; all decisions are delegated to the Application/Domain."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Time-based triggers, pollers, and saga handlers are incoming implementations and are therefore subject to the same constraints as other Transport Endpoints."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Mappings MUST be purely translational; they MUST NOT contain business logic (§5.2)."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Configuration logic MUST be purely mechanical wiring; it MUST NOT contain conditional business logic."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Host-level health checks and monitoring MAY be configured in Host Units but MUST remain operational concerns, not business behaviour."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Use Case services (defined in the Core) MUST be concrete and MUST NOT introduce a substitution abstraction; they are not intended to be mocked (§6.2)."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Coordination mechanisms (DI, mediator, command bus) MUST NOT affect Contract Models or Domain design (§8.1)."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A Host Unit MAY be packaged as the sole inbound implementation of a process or combined with other Host Units in a single process (AP-002 §1.1); in both cases startup is owned by a Deployment/Composition Unit entry point."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A process that composes multiple Host Units SHALL do so through a Composition Unit that contains no Transport Endpoints, Mappings, or business logic of its own (§5.7)."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Deployment Units SHALL remain orchestration-only: they compose Host registrations and infrastructure Adapter registrations, but do not implement Host or Adapter behaviour."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A Deployment/Composition Unit MAY deploy multiple Host Units bound to multiple Core Units."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A composed Host Unit SHALL expose its wiring as a callable Configuration registration so that it behaves identically whether run alone or composed (§5.7.2)."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A host-level liveness or health signal MUST remain an operational Configuration concern and MUST NOT invoke a Use Case or reach the Domain (§5.7.3)."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A transport-defined Host Unit SHALL remain bound to a single external technology; an internally-triggered Host Unit MAY publish multiple wirings for the same mechanism but MUST NOT combine different mechanisms (§5.1, §5.7.4)."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Deployment Units that package/combine Host Units SHOULD be kept separate from pure Host Units in repository structure (for example top-level deployment method projects alongside "},{"type":"text","text":"src/","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":") to avoid mixing composition concerns with incoming implementation concerns (§5.7.5)."}]}]}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"10. Compliance"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A service complies with this AP (§5.1)."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Each inbound trigger mechanism (such as scheduling, polling, or saga orchestration) is realized as a dedicated Host Unit where used (§5.1, §9)."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Host Units are organized into a transport-specific folder, "},{"type":"text","text":"Mappings/","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":", and "},{"type":"text","text":"Configuration/","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" registration, without a standalone application entry point (§5.1, §5.5)."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Transport Endpoints depend only on the Core's Application layer (§5.2, §9)."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Each Host Unit references exactly one Core Unit; the Core does not reference any Host Unit (§9)."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Transport Endpoints contain no business logic (§7.1, §9)."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Mappings contain no business logic (§7.2, §9)."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Use Cases are defined in the Core's "},{"type":"text","text":"Application/Contracts/","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" folder, not in Host Units (§6.3)."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Host Unit names follow the "},{"type":"text","text":"ServiceName.HostType","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" pattern (§5.6)."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Where multiple Host Units are deployed together, they are composed through a Composition Unit that adds no Transport Endpoints, Mappings, or business logic (§5.7)."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Deployment/Composition Units are orchestration-only and compose Host and infrastructure Adapter registrations without implementing endpoint or adapter behaviour."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Deployment/Composition Units MAY compose Host Units from one or many Core Units."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Deployment/composition projects are structurally separated from pure Host Units and kept as top-level deployment method projects outside "},{"type":"text","text":"src/","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":", so transport/trigger endpoint code remains in Host Units (§5.7.5)."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Host-level liveness and health signals are realized as operational Configuration and do not invoke (§5.7.4)."}]}]}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"11. "},{"type":"text","text":"Domain/","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":", "},{"type":"text","text":"SharedKernel/","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":", "},{"type":"text","text":"Capabilities/","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":", and "},{"type":"text","text":"Application/","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":", and how Authorize_Declines_When_Amount_Exceeds_Maximum()\n{\n    // Arrange\n    var fraudDetection = Substitute.For<IFraudDetectionService>();\n    fraudDetection.IsFraudulent(Arg.Any<decimal>(), Arg.Any<string>(), Arg.Any<string>())\n        .Returns(false);\n\n    var maximumAmount = new Money(10000, \"USD\");\n    var service = new PaymentAuthorizationService(fraudDetection, maximumAmount);\n    var amount = new Money(15000, \"USD\"); // Exceeds limit\n\n    // Act\n    var result = service.Authorize(amount, \"MERCH-001\");\n\n    // Assert\n    Assert.False(result.IsAuthorized);\n    Assert.Equal(PaymentStatus.Declined, result.Status);\n    Assert.Contains(\"exceeds maximum\", result.DeclineReason);\n}\n\n[Fact]\npublic void Authorize_Declines_When_Fraud_Detected()\n{\n    // Arrange\n    var fraudDetection = Substitute.For<IFraudDetectionService>();\n    fraudDetection.IsFraudulent(Arg.Any<decimal>(), Arg.Any<string>(), Arg.Any<string>())\n        .Returns(true);\n\n    var maximumAmount = new Money(10000, \"USD\");\n    var service = new PaymentAuthorizationService(fraudDetection, maximumAmount);\n    var amount = new Money(100, \"USD\");\n\n    // Act\n    var result = service.Authorize(amount, \"MERCH-001\");\n\n    // Assert\n    Assert.False(result.IsAuthorized);\n    Assert.Equal(PaymentStatus.Declined, result.Status);\n    Assert.Equal(\"Suspected fraud\", result.DeclineReason);\n}\n\n// Value Object test\n[Fact]\npublic void Money_Add_Returns_Correct_Total()\n{\n    var money1 = new Money(100.00m, \"USD\");\n    var money2 = new Money(50.00m, \"USD\");\n\n    var total = money1.Add(money2);\n\n    Assert.Equal(150.00m, total.Amount);\n    Assert.Equal(\"USD\", total.Currency);\n}\n\n// Use Case test with mocked repository\n[Fact]\npublic void AuthorizePayment_Saves_And_Returns_Result()\n{\n    // Arrange\n    var fraudDetection = Substitute.For<IFraudDetectionService>();\n    fraudDetection.IsFraudulent(Arg.Any<decimal>(), Arg.Any<string>(), Arg.Any<string>())\n        .Returns(false);\n\n    var repository = Substitute.For<IPaymentRepository>();\n    var savedId = Guid.NewGuid();\n    repository.Save(Arg.Any<Money>(), Arg.Any<string>(), Arg.Any<PaymentStatus>(),\n        Arg.Any<string?>())\n        .Returns(new PaymentRecord(\n            savedId, \n            new Money(100, \"USD\"), \n            \"MERCH-001\", \n            PaymentStatus.Authorized, \n            DateTime.UtcNow));\n\n    var maximumAmount = new Money(10000, \"USD\");\n    var authService = new PaymentAuthorizationService(fraudDetection, maximumAmount);\n    var useCase = new AuthorizePaymentUseCase(authService, repository);\n\n    var request = new AuthorizePaymentRequest(new Money(100, \"USD\"), \"MERCH-001\");\n\n    // Act\n    var response = useCase.Execute(request);\n\n    // Assert\n    Assert.True(response.IsAuthorized);\n    Assert.Equal(PaymentStatus.Authorized, response.Status);\n    Assert.Equal(savedId, response.PaymentId);\n}"}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"9. When to Add Entities"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"This stateless approach works well for simple operations. Consider introducing entity classes when:"}]},{"type":"orderedList","attrs":{"order":1},"content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Complex state transitions","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":": Multiple state changes with invariants that must be enforced"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Rich behavior","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":": Business logic that naturally belongs to the concept (not just validation)"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Aggregate boundaries","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":": Multiple related objects that must remain consistent"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Domain events","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":": Capturing what happened for integration or audit"}]}]}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"For many services, the stateless approach with Value Objects and external persistence is sufficient and simpler."}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"10. Session Script"}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"3.1 Definition"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A Session Script is the static, declarative definition of a coordinated flow. It is the single source of truth for how Steps compose."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A Session Script MUST include:"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A "},{"type":"text","text":"ScriptId","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":"."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A human-readable "},{"type":"text","text":"ScriptVersion","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":"."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"An ordered set of Steps and their allowed transitions."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"For each Step, the global message identifiers it consumes and produces."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"For each Step, the global data-element identifiers it requires as input and produces as output (see Section 6)."}]}]}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A Session Script MUST be versioned and immutable once published."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A ("},{"type":"text","text":"Activation","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":", "},{"type":"text","text":"Rollback","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":", "},{"type":"text","text":"Done","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":", "},{"type":"text","text":"Failed","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":")."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"These identifiers are global to the flow so that a Step's outputs can be matched to the next Step's inputs without direct coupling between Participants."}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"3.3 Start Declaration"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A Session Script MAY declare a "},{"type":"text","text":"start signal","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" and its trigger binding, which governs how a Session for this script is initiated (Session Initiation, SOPC-001 §5.8)."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Where declared, the start declaration MUST identify:"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The global message identifier of the start signal that activates an initiation poll."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The identity of the first Step activated when a Session is created."}]}]}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The trigger condition itself (user action, timer, or other environmental condition) is evaluated by the initiator and is not part of the immutable script beyond the declared start signal it responds to. A Session Script that declares no start signal relies on an out-of-band initiator to create Sessions."}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"4. Session Script Interface (Abstract)"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The Session Script is exposed through an abstract interface. The interface is normative; the concrete implementation is a separate library project."}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"4.1 Requirements"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"An implementation of the Session Script interface MUST:"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Be immutable once loaded for a given "},{"type":"text","text":"(ScriptId, ScriptVersion)","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":"."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Be free of runtime session data (the interface exposes definition, not state)."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Be safe for concurrent read access by multiple Participants."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Report structural and dataflow validity (Section 6) before it is considered publishable."}]}]}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"4.2 Methods"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The interface SHALL expose at least the following methods, whose semantics are normative (signatures below are illustrative, language-neutral):"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Identity() -> (ScriptId, ScriptVersion, Name)","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" — returns the immutable identity of the script."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Steps() -> [StepId]","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" — returns the ordered set of Steps."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Transitions(StepId) -> [Transition]","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" — returns allowed successor transitions for a Step, including explicit failure and compensation transitions."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"MessageBindings(StepId) -> { Activation, Rollback, Done, Failed }","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" — returns the global message identifiers bound to a Step's four coordination messages."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Inputs(StepId) -> [DataElementId]","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" — returns the global data-element identifiers the Step requires as input."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Outputs(StepId) -> [DataElementId]","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" — returns the global data-element identifiers the Step produces."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Classification(DataElementId) -> Classification","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" — returns the data classification for a data element."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Validate() -> ValidationResult","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" — performs the structural and dataflow validation of Section 6."}]}]}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"All methods MUST be deterministic and side-effect free."}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"5. Deployment Interface"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Deployment of a Session Script is exposed through a separate deployment interface, distinct from the read-only Session Script interface of Section 4."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"An implementation of the deployment interface MUST:"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Distribute(Script, [Target])","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" — deliver a Session Script to every participating service at deployment time."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Verify(Target) -> VerificationResult","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" — confirm that a target holds a "},{"type":"text","text":"ScriptVersion","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" compatible with the Steps it implements, failing deployment on mismatch."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"ActiveVersions() -> [(ScriptId, ScriptVersion)]","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" — enumerate versions currently deployed, so in-flight Sessions can continue on their originating version."}]}]}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The deployment interface MUST NOT mutate a published Session Script; version changes are always expressed as a new "},{"type":"text","text":"ScriptVersion","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":"."}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"6. Versioning and Deployment Distribution"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A Session Script SHALL be distributed to every service participating in the coordination at deployment time."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Each participating service MUST verify, at deployment, that it holds a Session Script version compatible with the Steps it implements. A version mismatch detected at deployment MUST fail the deployment or be surfaced as an explicit operational error."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Every coordination message MUST carry the "},{"type":"text","text":"ScriptId","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" and "},{"type":"text","text":"ScriptVersion","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" so mismatches are detectable at runtime, not only at deployment. A Participant that receives an "},{"type":"text","text":"Activation","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" referencing a "},{"type":"text","text":"ScriptVersion","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" it does not recognize MUST emit "},{"type":"text","text":"Failed","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" (fail-fast) rather than execute against an assumed definition."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Breaking changes to a Session Script MUST be published as a new "},{"type":"text","text":"ScriptVersion","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":". In-flight Sessions MUST continue to run against the "},{"type":"text","text":"ScriptVersion","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" under which they were started."}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"7. Coordination and Transitions"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Transitions are inferred from the Session Script and observed Session Log events; no mandatory central runtime drives them."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A transition to the next Step SHALL occur only after the required predecessor Step outcomes ("},{"type":"text","text":"Done","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":") are present in the Session Log."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Failure transitions MUST be explicit in the Session Script."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Compensation transitions ("},{"type":"text","text":"Rollback","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":", per SOPC-001 §5.4) SHOULD be defined for every Step that produces externally visible side effects, and MUST define the reverse order in which completed predecessor Steps are compensated."}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"7.1 Session Coordinator"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The runtime role that observes the Session Log, consults the Session Script, and emits the next "},{"type":"text","text":"Activation","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" (or "},{"type":"text","text":"Rollback","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":") is the "},{"type":"text","text":"Session Coordinator","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":"."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The Session Coordinator MUST be "},{"type":"text","text":"flow-agnostic","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":": it is a generic driver parameterized entirely by the Session Script. It MUST NOT embed flow-specific logic — no per-flow state enumeration and no per-flow branching. All flow knowledge lives in the Session Script (data); the Coordinator only reads the Script and the Session Log to decide what happens next."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The Session Coordinator MAY be realized in either of two forms, and this choice is an implementation concern:"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Distributed","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" — each Participant reacts to predecessor "},{"type":"text","text":"Done","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" events and self-activates its Steps, with no central component."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Centralized","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" — a single generic driver reads the Session Log and emits the next activation."}]}]}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A centralized Session Coordinator is permitted precisely because it carries no flow-specific logic; it is therefore not a central orchestrator in the sense SOPC deprecates (SOPC-001 §3, SOPC-002 §11). A component that embeds flow-specific transitions is not a Session Coordinator but a reintroduction of the central-coordinator anti-pattern."}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"8. Global Data-Element Mapping"}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"8.1 Purpose"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The Session Script MUST declare, per Step, the global data-element identifiers the Step requires as input and the identifiers it produces as output. This turns the script into a dataflow contract in addition to a control-flow contract, enabling a bird's-eye view of what each Step consumes and produces."}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"8.2 Constraints"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The mapping MUST reference data elements by global identifier and classification only. It MUST NOT define the internal value structure of a data element; that remains a Step concern per SOPC-001 §7.1. Encoding of the data element on the wire and at rest follows Section 9."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Publication of a Session Script MUST perform a static dataflow validation: every required input identifier for a Step MUST be produced by some predecessor Step in the flow. A Session Script that fails this validation MUST NOT be published."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Data classification attached to a global data-element identifier MAY drive redaction and least-privilege rules for derived views (SOPC-001 §10)."}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"9. Global Data-Element Definitions"}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"9.1 Globally Unique Identity"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Every data element in the flow MUST be identified by a "},{"type":"text","text":"globally unique identifier","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" ("},{"type":"text","text":"DataElementId","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":"). The identifier scheme MUST guarantee uniqueness without central coordination; a UUID is a suitable choice, but the specific scheme is left to the implementation."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A "},{"type":"text","text":"DataElementId","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" is the single reference used everywhere the element appears: in the Session Script mapping (Section 8), in "},{"type":"text","text":"Query","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" responses (Section 10), and in any serialized form."}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"9.2 Rules"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A data element MAY be "},{"type":"text","text":"primitive","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" (a single value) or "},{"type":"text","text":"composite","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" (a set of nested, individually identified data elements)."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A reader MUST ignore data elements whose identifier it does not recognize rather than fail, preserving forward compatibility (per SOPC-001 §6.2 schema evolution)."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The identifier used in any serialized form MUST be the same "},{"type":"text","text":"DataElementId","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" declared in the Session Script data-element mapping (Section 8), so that the data is self-describing against the script's bird's-eye view."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Any serialized representation is a wire and at-rest form only; the normative state contract remains the typed value objects of SOPC-001 §7.1. The concrete serialization is an implementation detail. A self-describing, length-delimited encoding keyed by the global identifier — for example, a BER-TLV-style tag/length/value structure in the spirit of EMV — is a reasonable direction, but no specific byte format is mandated by this RFC."}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"10. Query and Derivation"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The "},{"type":"text","text":"Query","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" message (SOPC-001 §5.3, §7.3) returns Step-local data by global data-element identifier. Because the Session Script declares which identifiers each Step produces, a caller can derive a complete Session data view by collecting the declared outputs across Steps."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Derived business artifacts (for example, receipt or ticket views) MAY be produced from projections over the Session Log, using the Session Script's data-element declarations as the schema of available fields."}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"11. Relationship to a Central Coordinator (Non-Normative)"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"This section is non-normative. It records the design intent that the Session Script "},{"type":"text","text":"replaces a central coordinator","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" — a single, ever-growing state machine that owns every transition of a flow. Such a monolith is the tight-coupling anti-pattern SOPC-001 §3 sets out to remove."}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"11.1 The Mapping"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A central coordinator typically concentrates several concerns in one place. Under SOPC these are separated:"}]},{"type":"table","attrs":{"layout":"default"},"content":[{"type":"tableRow","content":[{"type":"tableHeader","attrs":{"colspan":1,"rowspan":1,"colwidth":[340]},"content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Central coordinator concern"}]}]},{"type":"tableHeader","attrs":{"colspan":1,"rowspan":1,"colwidth":[340]},"content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"SOPC replacement"}]}]}]},{"type":"tableRow","content":[{"type":"tableCell","attrs":{"colspan":1,"rowspan":1,"colwidth":[340]},"content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"One global state enumeration"}]}]},{"type":"tableCell","attrs":{"colspan":1,"rowspan":1,"colwidth":[340]},"content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Session Script Steps and transitions (§3, §7)"}]}]}]},{"type":"tableRow","content":[{"type":"tableCell","attrs":{"colspan":1,"rowspan":1,"colwidth":[340]},"content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"One dispatch switch owning all sequencing"}]}]},{"type":"tableCell","attrs":{"colspan":1,"rowspan":1,"colwidth":[340]},"content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Coordination inferred from Script + Session Log (§7); no central runtime"}]}]}]},{"type":"tableRow","content":[{"type":"tableCell","attrs":{"colspan":1,"rowspan":1,"colwidth":[340]},"content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"An idle/start state that begins new work"}]}]},{"type":"tableCell","attrs":{"colspan":1,"rowspan":1,"colwidth":[340]},"content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Initiator, a small runtime role (SOPC-001 §5.8)"}]}]}]},{"type":"tableRow","content":[{"type":"tableCell","attrs":{"colspan":1,"rowspan":1,"colwidth":[340]},"content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A shared mutable state structure threaded through every branch"}]}]},{"type":"tableCell","attrs":{"colspan":1,"rowspan":1,"colwidth":[340]},"content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Session data, copied by value into declared data-elements (SOPC-001 §7.5, §8)"}]}]}]},{"type":"tableRow","content":[{"type":"tableCell","attrs":{"colspan":1,"rowspan":1,"colwidth":[340]},"content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Sub-state-machines invoked by the switch"}]}]},{"type":"tableCell","attrs":{"colspan":1,"rowspan":1,"colwidth":[340]},"content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Steps / Participants (SOPC-001 §5.3, §5.6)"}]}]}]},{"type":"tableRow","content":[{"type":"tableCell","attrs":{"colspan":1,"rowspan":1,"colwidth":[340]},"content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Long-lived device/health handling"}]}]},{"type":"tableCell","attrs":{"colspan":1,"rowspan":1,"colwidth":[340]},"content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Health Capability (SOPC-001 §5.7)"}]}]}]}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"11.2 Where Reuse Comes From"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Existing sub-state-machines are usually already close to Steps: each has its own internal states and a terminal outcome that maps onto "},{"type":"text","text":"Done","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" or "},{"type":"text","text":"Failed","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":". What binds them into a monolith is not their logic but two couplings:"}]},{"type":"orderedList","attrs":{"order":1},"content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"a shared mutable state structure, and"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"a central switch that owns all sequencing."}]}]}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Removing both — shared state via copy-by-value data-elements, sequencing via the Session Script — allows each sub-state-machine to become an independently deployable, reusable Step. Device-access and health code is reused as-is, reframed as a Health Capability plus device access."}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"11.3 Incremental Migration"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Deprecating a central coordinator need not be a single cutover. A flow MAY be migrated one Step at a time: extract a sub-state-machine behind the four coordination messages ("},{"type":"text","text":"Activation","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":", "},{"type":"text","text":"Rollback","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":", "},{"type":"text","text":"Done","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":", "},{"type":"text","text":"Failed","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":") and let the Session Script sequence it, while the legacy coordinator still drives the remaining, not-yet-extracted portions. As Steps are extracted, the central state enumeration shrinks toward the initiator's idle role and is finally retired."}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"12. Open Questions"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Genuine spec-level questions:"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Should a Start Declaration (§3.3) be mandatory for self-contained scripts, or remain optional to allow out-of-band initiators?"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Should the Session Script mandate parallel execution of independent (non-dependent) Steps, or leave concurrency to the implementation?"}]}]}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Deferred to the Session Script \"manager\" implementation, not this RFC:"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Session Script serialization format (JSON Schema, YAML) and whether it is exposed to callers."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The transport used by the deployment interface for "},{"type":"text","text":"Distribute","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":"."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Ordered delivery guarantees per Session partition key."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Projection storage pattern for derived views (a general persistence concern, e.g. receipt and ticket generation)."}]}]}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"13. References"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"[1] S. All business decisions\n///       are made in the domain layer.\n/// \n/// @see application::use_cases::AuthorizePaymentUseCase\n/// @see application::use_cases::GetPaymentUseCase\nclass PaymentsController {\npublic:\n    explicit PaymentsController(application::container::ServiceContainer* container);\n\n    /// @brief POST /api/payments/authorize\n    /// @details Authorizes a payment request\n    /// @param request HTTP request containing payment details (JSON)\n    /// @return HTTP 200 with authorization result, or 400 on invalid request\n    /// \n    /// Request JSON:\n    /// @code{.json}\n    /// {\n    ///   \"amount\": 100.00,\n    ///   \"currency\": \"USD\",\n    ///   \"merchantId\": \"MERCH-001\"\n    /// }\n    /// @endcode\n    /// \n    /// Response JSON:\n    /// @code{.json}\n    /// {\n    ///   \"paymentId\": \"uuid\",\n    ///   \"isAuthorized\": true,\n    ///   \"status\": \"Authorized\",\n    ///   \"amount\": 100.00,\n    ///   \"currency\": \"USD\"\n    /// }\n    /// @endcode\n    /// \n    /// @see models::AuthorizePaymentDto\n    /// @see application::contracts::AuthorizePaymentRequest\n    QHttpServerResponse AuthorizePayment(const QHttpServerRequest& request);\n\n    /// @brief GET /api/payments/{id}\n    /// @details Retrieves payment details by ID\n    /// @param payment_id Payment identifier\n    /// @return HTTP 200 with payment details, or 404 if not found\n    /// \n    /// @see models::PaymentDetailsDto\n    /// @see application::contracts::GetPaymentResponse\n    QHttpServerResponse
