Implementation"}]},{"type":"codeBlock","attrs":{"language":"cpp"},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"// mypaymentservice_http/controllers/payments_controller.cpp\n#include \"payments_controller.h\"\n#include \"models/payment_dto.h\"\n#include \"domain/value_objects/money.h\"\n#include <QJsonDocument>\n#include <QJsonObject>\n\nnamespace mypaymentservice::http::controllers {\n\nPaymentsController::PaymentsController(application::container::ServiceContainer* container) {\n    // Resolve use cases from container\n    authorize_use_case_ = container->Resolve<application::use_cases::AuthorizePaymentUseCase>();\n    get_use_case_ = container->Resolve<application::use_cases::GetPaymentUseCase>();\n}\n\nQHttpServerResponse PaymentsController::AuthorizePayment(const QHttpServerRequest& request) {\n    // Parse JSON body\n    QJsonDocument doc = QJsonDocument::fromJson(request.body());\n    if (!doc.isObject()) {\n        return QHttpServerResponse(\"application/json\", \n                                   QJsonDocument(QJsonObject{{\"error\", \"Invalid JSON\"}}).toJson(),\n                                   QHttpServerResponse::StatusCode::BadRequest);\n    }\n\n    // Map HTTP DTO from JSON\n    auto dto_opt = models::AuthorizePaymentDto::FromJson(doc.object());\n    if (!dto_opt) {\n        return QHttpServerResponse(\"application/json\",\n                                   QJsonDocument(QJsonObject{{\"error\", \"Missing required fields\"}}).toJson(),\n                                   QHttpServerResponse::StatusCode::BadRequest);\n    }\n    auto dto = *dto_opt;\n\n    // Map HTTP DTO -> Contract Model (using shared Value Objects)\n    application::contracts::AuthorizePaymentRequest use_case_request{\n        domain::value_objects::Money(dto.amount, dto.currency.toStdString()),\n        dto.merchant_id.toStdString()\n    };\n\n    // Execute use case (synchronous, short-lived thread)\n    auto result = authorize_use_case_->Execute(use_case_request);\n\n    // Map result -> HTTP DTO\n    models::PaymentAuthorizationDto response_dto;\n    response_dto.payment_id = QString::fromStdString(result.id);\n    response_dto.is_authorized = result.is_authorized;\n    response_dto.status = QString::fromStdString(\n        PaymentStatusToString(result.status));\n    response_dto.amount = result.amount.GetAmount();\n    response_dto.currency = QString::fromStdString(result.amount.GetCurrency());\n\n    if (result.decline_reason) {\n        response_dto.decline_reason = QString::fromStdString(*result.decline_reason);\n    }\n\n    // Return JSON response\n    QJsonDocument response_doc(response_dto.ToJson());\n    return QHttpServerResponse(\"application/json\", \n                               response_doc.toJson(),\n                               QHttpServerResponse::StatusCode::Ok);\n}\n\nQHttpServerResponse PaymentsController::GetPayment(const QString& payment_id) {\n    // Execute use case\n    auto result = get_use_case_->Execute(payment_id.toStdString());\n\n    if (!result) {\n        return QHttpServerResponse(\"application/json\",\n                                   QJsonDocument(QJsonObject{{\"error\", \"Payment not found\"}}).toJson(),\n                                   QHttpServerResponse::StatusCode::NotFound);\n    }\n\n    // Map result -> HTTP DTO\n    models::PaymentDetailsDto response_dto;\n    response_dto.payment_id = QString::fromStdString(result->id);\n    response_dto.amount = result->amount.GetAmount();\n    response_dto.currency = QString::fromStdString(result->amount.GetCurrency());\n    response_dto.merchant_id = QString::fromStdString(result->merchant_id);\n    response_dto.status = QString::fromStdString(PaymentStatusToString(result->status));\n    response_dto.created_at = QDateTime::fromSecsSinceEpoch(\n        std::chrono::system_clock::to_time_t(result->created_at));\n\n    if (result->decline_reason) {\n        response_dto.decline_reason = QString::fromStdString(*result->decline_reason);\n    }\n\n    // Return JSON response\n    QJsonDocument response_doc(response_dto.ToJson());\n    return QHttpServerResponse(\"application/json\",\n                               response_doc.toJson(),\n                               QHttpServerResponse::StatusCode::Ok);\n}\n\n// Helper to convert PaymentStatus enum to string\nstd::string PaymentsController::PaymentStatusToString(\n    domain::value_objects::PaymentStatus status) {\n    switch (status) {\n        case domain::value_objects::PaymentStatus::Pending:\n            return \"Pending\";\n        case domain::value_objects::PaymentStatus::Authorized:\n            return \"Authorized\";\n        case domain::value_objects::PaymentStatus::Declined:\n            return \"Declined\";\n        default:\n            return \"Unknown\";\n    }\n}\n\n} // namespace mypaymentservice::http::controllers"}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"5. Lightweight HTTP handling"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Controller responsibility","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":": Map HTTP -> Contract Models only"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Use Case invocation","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":": Direct, synchronous call"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Thread lifecycle","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":": Short-lived request-response"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Separation","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":": HTTP DTOs -> Contract Models -> Value Objects"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Direct DI","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":": Service container provides dependencies"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Documentation","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":": Doxygen + manual mapping document"}]}]}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The pattern is simple: receive request -> parse JSON -> map to contract -> invoke use case -> map to JSON -> return response."}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"See Also:","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"C++ Implementation (AP-002)","marks":[{"type":"link","attrs":{"href":"#"}}]},{"type":"text","text":" - Introduction"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"This AP defines the architectural principles governing the structure of all software services within this ecosystem."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The objective is to establish a technology-independent architecture that promotes maintainability, testability, portability, and long-term consistency."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"This AP defines architectural invariants only. It does not prescribe implementation technologies, deployment models, or programming languages."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Detailed structural requirements are specified in subsequent APs."}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"2. Normative Language"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The key words "},{"type":"text","text":"MUST","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":", "},{"type":"text","text":"MUST NOT","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":", "},{"type":"text","text":"SHALL","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":", "},{"type":"text","text":"SHOULD","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":", and "},{"type":"text","text":"MAY","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" in this document are to be interpreted as described in BCP 14 (RFC 2119 [1] and RFC 8174 [2]) when, and only when, they appear in all capitals, as shown here."}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"3. Terminology"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"This section defines the terms used throughout this AP."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Service","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":": An independently deployable unit of software composed of an Application, a Domain, a Shared Kernel, and the Domain's Capabilities."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Domain","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":": The architectural area that expresses pure business meaning, state, and rules."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Capability","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":": A domain-owned abstraction describing something the Domain requires, expressed in domain terms and independent of any technology."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Core","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":": The Application, the Domain, the Shared Kernel, and their Capabilities."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Shared Kernel","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":": A small, disciplined set of shared immutable business datatypes used across multiple areas of the Core without changing their business meaning."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Implementation","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":": A technology-specific realization that either receives stimuli for the Application or fulfills a Capability using concrete technology. Implementations exist outside the Core architectural model."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Deployment","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":": A thin coordinating layer that composes one or more Implementations into a runnable process. Deployment is to Implementations what Application is to Domain: it coordinates, but does not own business rules."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Entity","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":": An entity is an object that represents something unique in the business. It keeps the same identity throughout its lifetime, even though its properties may change."},{"type":"hardBreak"},{"type":"text","text":"Example: A customer may change name, address, or phone number, but it is still the same customer. [3]."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Value Object","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":": A value object represents a descriptive value in the business. It is defined entirely by its attributes, so two value objects with the same values are considered equal [3]. Shared Value Objects that are reused across multiple areas of the Core belong to the Shared Kernel (Section 6.4)."},{"type":"hardBreak"},{"type":"text","text":"Example: Two Money(100, \"EUR\") objects represent the same value, even if they were created at different times."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Domain Service","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":": A stateless domain operation expressing business behaviour that does not naturally belong to a single Entity or Value Object [3]."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Contract Model","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":": A language-neutral structure describing an input, output, or message exchanged with the Application. Contract Models include Request Models, Response Models, and Message Models."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Business Decision","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":": A determination of a business outcome governed by business rules (for example, whether a reservation is allowed or a payment is authorised), as distinct from a technical operation."}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"4. Motivation"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Modern software frequently couples business logic to transport protocols, infrastructure technologies, and deployment environments."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Such coupling commonly results in:"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Reduced testability."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Increased deployment complexity."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Difficult technology migration."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Large end-to-end test suites."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Inconsistent service implementations."}]}]}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"This architecture separates business behaviour from implementation concerns, allowing business functionality to be validated independently of deployment."}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"4.1 Logical Architecture vs. Physical Deployment"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"This architecture defines "},{"type":"text","text":"logical separation","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" of concerns, not physical deployment topology."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The architectural benefits of microservices — modularity, independent testability, clear boundaries, technology independence — can be achieved through logical structure "},{"type":"text","text":"without incurring the operational overhead of distributed deployment","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":"."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"As Martin Fowler argues in writings such as "},{"type":"text","text":"MonolithFirst","marks":[{"type":"em"}]},{"type":"text","text":" [12] and "},{"type":"text","text":"Microservice Trade-Offs","marks":[{"type":"em"}]},{"type":"text","text":" [11], microservices introduce substantial qualitative costs: distributed transaction complexity, network latency, operational monitoring overhead, duplicated resources, and greater coordination effort. These costs are justified only when independent scalability, organizational autonomy, or deployment independence require physical separation."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"This architecture follows the principle: "},{"type":"text","text":"make it work, make it right, make it fast","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" (attributed to Kent Beck, often paraphrased from Knuth's \"premature optimization\" principle). The logical structure defined here provides modularity and testability (make it work, make it right) without mandating distributed deployment (make it fast — only when needed)."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A service structured according to these principles:"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"MAY be deployed as a single process (monolith)"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"MAY be deployed as multiple independent processes (microservices)"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"MAY share resources (database, message bus) with other services"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"MAY use dedicated resources for independent scaling"}]}]}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Deployment topology is an optimization decision","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":", not an architectural requirement. A well-structured monolith with strict boundaries is therefore the default economical starting point; the architecture enables later physical separation when justified by operational needs, but does not impose it prematurely."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The logical boundaries defined in this AP — Application, Domain, Shared Kernel, Capabilities, Implementations, and Deployment — remain invariant regardless of deployment model."}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"5. Architectural Model"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Every service SHALL be composed of the following logical areas within the (domain-owned abstractions)\n└── Application"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Implementations and Deployment exist outside this Core architectural "},{"type":"text","text":"group things by the reason they change, and compose those groups at the next higher Application"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The Application directs the use cases of the service."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The Application Domain"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The Domain defines business meaning [3]."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The Domain SHALL distinguish rules."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Policies."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Domain Services."}]}]}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The architecture primarily protects business behaviour. Entities, Value Objects, and Domain Services are the tactical patterns of Domain-Driven Design [3], [4]. Shared immutable Value Objects that are reused across multiple areas of the Core MAY belong to the Shared Kernel (Section 6.4)."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The Domain Capabilities"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A Capability is an abstract description of something the Domain requires to fulfil its business rules."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Capabilities are owned by the Domain and expressed in domain terms. They describe "},{"type":"text","text":"what","marks":[{"type":"em"}]},{"type":"text","text":" the Domain requires, never "},{"type":"text","text":"how","marks":[{"type":"em"}]},{"type":"text","text":" it is provided."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Typical capabilities 7)."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Capabilities correspond to the driven (secondary) ports of Hexagonal Architecture [7] and apply the Dependency Inversion Principle [9]."}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"7. Implementations and Deployment"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Implementations interact with the Application or realize Capabilities using specific technologies."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Examples include:"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"HTTP endpoints."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Message subscribers."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Database implementations."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Message broker integrations."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Scheduled tasks."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Command-line interfaces."}]}]}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Deployment coordinates one or more Implementations into a runnable process."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"This relationship mirrors the Core split: Deployment is to Implementations what Application is to Domain — a thin orchestration layer over behaviour-bearing units."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Implementations MAY depend upon any Core architectural component. Deployment MAY depend upon Core components and Implementations for composition."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Core architectural components MUST NOT depend upon Implementations or Deployment."}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"8. Dependency Rules"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Dependencies SHALL always move toward business meaning."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Business meaning resides in the Domain. The Application, Domain, Shared Kernel, and their Capabilities form the Core. All Implementations and Deployment depend upon the Core; the Core depends upon nothing outside itself."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"This is the Dependency Rule of Clean Architecture [5] and an application of the Dependency Inversion Principle [9]."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The following dependency directions are permitted:"}]},{"type":"codeBlock","attrs":{"language":"text"},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"Implementations  ──▶  Application + Domain + Shared Kernel + Capabilities\nDeployment       ──▶  Implementations"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The following dependencies are prohibited:"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Core to Implementations or Deployment."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Application, Domain, or Shared Kernel to transport technologies."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Application, Domain, or Shared Kernel to persistence technologies."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Capabilities to implementation technologies."}]}]}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"9. Architectural Smells"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The following are common violations of this architecture. Their presence indicates that a principle defined in this AP has been broken."}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Business decisions made outside the Domain."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Transport-specific types appearing in the Domain."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Duplicate transport-neutral models introduced where a Shared Kernel would preserve a single business language."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Capability implementations referenced by the Domain."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Business rules duplicated across multiple implementations."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Framework types leaking into architectural components."}]}]}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"This section provides practical review guidance; it does not introduce new requirements beyond those already defined."}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"10. Evolution"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"This AP defines logical responsibilities only. It intentionally does not prescribe how those responsibilities are physically organized."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Subsequent APs SHALL define:"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Service structure."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Folder layout."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Naming conventions."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Dependency rules for concrete units."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Testing strategy."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Implementation guidance."}]}]}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Such APs SHALL remain consistent with the invariants defined here, foremost the Decision Ownership invariant (Section 6.1)."}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"11. Testability"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Business behaviour SHALL be executable without deployment."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Business verification MUST NOT require:"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Network communication."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Databases."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Message brokers."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"External services."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Containers."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Cloud infrastructure."}]}]}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Implementation-specific behaviour SHALL be verified independently from business behaviour."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"End-to-end testing SHOULD primarily validate deployment, integration, and configuration."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"This testing strategy is consistent with the test pyramid [10]. A full testing strategy is defined in AP-008."}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"12. Design Goals"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"This architecture is intended to achieve:"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Independent evolution of business logic and technology."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Consistent service structure."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"High unit and component test coverage."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Minimal deployment dependencies during development."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Replaceable implementation technologies."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Clear ownership of responsibilities."}]}]}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"13. Compliance"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A service complies with this AP if:"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Every business decision is made within exactly one Domain."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"All business logic resides within the Domain."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The Application contains no business behaviour."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The Shared Kernel contains only stable immutable business datatypes and remains technology-independent."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Capabilities remain technology-independent abstractions."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Implementations are technology-specific and isolated from Core architectural definitions."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Deployment remains a thin coordinating layer and contains no business rules."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Business functionality can be executed independently of deployment technologies."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Dependency rules defined in this AP are satisfied."}]}]}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"14. i < argc; ++i) {\n        std::string arg = argv[i];\n\n        if (arg.substr(0, 2) == \"--\") {\n            // Option with value: --amount 100\n            if (i + 1 < argc && argv[i + 1][0] != '-') {\n                options_[arg.substr(2)] = argv[i + 1];\n                ++i;\n            } else {\n                // Flag: --help\n                flags_.push_back(arg.substr(2));\n            }\n        } else {\n            // Positional argument\n            positional_args_.push_back(arg);\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nstd::string CliParser::GetCommand() const {\n    return command_;\n}\n\nbool CliParser::HasFlag(const std::string& flag) const {\n    return std::find(flags_.begin(), flags_.end(), flag) != flags_.end();\n}\n\nstd::string CliParser::GetOption(const std::string& option) const {\n    auto it = options_.find(option);\n    return it != options_.end() ? it->second : \"\";\n}\n\nstd::string CliParser::GetPositional(size_t index) const {\n    return index < positional_args_.size() ? positional_args_[index] : \"\";\n}\n\nbool CliParser::IsValid() const {\n    return !command_.empty();\n}\n\n} // namespace mypaymentservice::cli"}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"4. application::use_cases::AuthorizePaymentUseCase* use_case)\n    : use_case_(use_case) {\n}\n\nint AuthorizeCommand::Execute(const CliParser& parser) {\n    if (parser.HasFlag(\"help\")) {\n        PrintUsage();\n        return 0;\n    }\n\n    // Parse CLI arguments\n    std::string amount_str = parser.GetOption(\"amount\");\n    std::string currency = parser.GetOption(\"currency\");\n    std::string merchant_id = parser.GetOption(\"merchant\");\n\n    // Validate required arguments\n    if (amount_str.empty() || currency.empty() || merchant_id.empty()) {\n        std::cerr << \"Error: << \"Error: Invalid amount: \" << amount_str << \"\\n\";\n        return 1;\n    }\n\n    // Map CLI arguments -> Contract Model\n    application::contracts::AuthorizePaymentRequest request{\n        domain::value_objects::Money(amount, currency),\n        merchant_id\n    };\n\n    // Execute use case (synchronous)\n    auto result = use_case_->Execute(request);\n\n    // Output result to console\n    std::cout << \"\\n\";\n    std::cout << \"=== Payment Authorization Result ===\\n\";\n    std::cout << \"Payment ID:     \" << result.id << \"\\n\";\n    std::cout << \"Authorized:     \" domain::value_objects::PaymentStatus::Pending:\n            return \"Pending\";\n        case domain::value_objects::PaymentStatus::Authorized:\n            return \"Authorized\";\n        case domain::value_objects::PaymentStatus::Declined:\n            return \"Declined\";\n        default:\n            return \"Unknown\";\n    }\n}\n\n} // namespace Terminology"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The logical term "},{"type":"text","text":"Capability","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" retains the meaning defined in AP-001 [3]: a domain-owned abstraction describing something the Domain requires, expressed in domain terms and independent of any technology."}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Capability Abstraction","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" — The interface (Port) that expresses a Capability in domain terms. Introduction"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"This RFC defines the "},{"type":"text","text":"core model","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" for coordinating long-running, stateful interactions across UI, device, and distributed service boundaries."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The model generalizes Saga-style behavior without introducing a mandatory central orchestrator. Coordination is driven by append-only session events and local participant logic."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"This document establishes the basics: the Step, its message lifecycle, the Session Log, and the local Session data model. Normative Language"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The key words "},{"type":"text","text":"MUST","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":", "},{"type":"text","text":"MUST NOT","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":", "},{"type":"text","text":"SHALL","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":", "},{"type":"text","text":"SHOULD","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":", and "},{"type":"text","text":"MAY","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" in this document are to be interpreted as described in BCP 14 (RFC 2119 and RFC 8174) when, and only when, they appear in all capitals, as shown here."}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"3. Motivation"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Distributed flows often become tightly coupled when one central runtime controls all step transitions."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"SOPC aims to preserve local autonomy while keeping end-to-end flow semantics explicit, auditable, and recoverable."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A Step is defined not by internal implementation but by the "},{"type":"text","text":"messages it subscribes to","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" and the "},{"type":"text","text":"messages it produces","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":". This keeps Steps autonomous and independently deployable."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"This model is intended to replace a central coordinator — a single, ever-growing state machine that owns every transition — with declared coordination; see the non-normative migration guidance in SOPC-002 §11."}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"4. Scope"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"In scope for this Core Model document:"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A runtime Session model for stateful execution."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The Step message lifecycle: activation, completion, failure, compensation, and data query."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"An append-only, versioned Session Log as the shared coordination surface."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A storage-agnostic model for locally persisted Session data."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Retention and persistence requirements for that data."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The term Health Entity, as a free-standing, identified entity separate from the flow model, whose state changes via messages consumed by a health agent."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Deployment profiles spanning single-process and distributed realizations of the same abstraction."}]}]}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Out of scope (see SOPC-002):"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The Session Script (static flow definition), its versioning, and deployment distribution."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Transition and coordination semantics between Steps."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Declaration of global data-element identifiers per Step."}]}]}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Out of scope services."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A mandated broker, log, or serialization technology."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"UI rendering details or transport-specific API contracts."}]}]}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"5. Core Concepts"}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"5.1 Session (Runtime)"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A Session represents one execution instance of a Session Script."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The runtime identity of a Session is minimal. A Session SHALL include only:"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"SessionId","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" — the identity of this concrete execution instance."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A reference to the governing Session Script, sufficient to resolve it: "},{"type":"text","text":"(ScriptId, ScriptVersion)","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":"."}]}]}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"All other properties a Session appears to \"have\" — its steps, their message names, and their input/output parameters — are not stored on the Session. They are "},{"type":"text","text":"derived","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" by resolving the Session Script (definition) against the Session Log (history). Values used only to verify or validate the script at deployment time are a deployment concern and are not published to the application runtime."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A Session MUST be reconstructable from its Session Log together with its Session Script."}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"5.2 Step Context (Self-Location)"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"When a Step is activated as part of a Session, it MUST be able to locate itself within the flow using only the identifiers carried on the "},{"type":"text","text":"Activation","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":": the "},{"type":"text","text":"SessionId","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":", the "},{"type":"text","text":"(ScriptId, ScriptVersion)","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":", and its own "},{"type":"text","text":"StepId","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" (step name)."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Given these, a Step SHALL resolve, from the Session Script:"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The message names bound to its actions A Step MUST NOT be relied upon as a long-lived owner of hardware or interface health, because it is not guaranteed to be resident between activations. Long-lived hardware and interface health is modeled separately as Health Entities (Section 5.7)."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A Step implementation MUST be idempotent for every message it consumes."}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"5.4 Step Messages"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A Step participates through "},{"type":"text","text":"five","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" message types:"}]},{"type":"orderedList","attrs":{"order":1},"content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Activation","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" — inbound. Requests the Step to compensate a previously completed execution for a Session."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Done","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" — outbound. Signals a terminal successful outcome."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Failed","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" — outbound. "},{"type":"text","text":"Failed","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":"."}]}]}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Compensation is itself an activation of a compensating action and therefore MUST terminate in "},{"type":"text","text":"Done","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" or Long-lived health is therefore modeled as a set of "},{"type":"text","text":"Health Entities","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":": free-standing, addressable entities decoupled from the Step lifecycle and from any single Session, rather than being bundled into a device handler."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A Health Entity SHALL have:"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A "},{"type":"text","text":"unique, stable identifier","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" that names the monitored component (for example, a device, interface, or subsystem) and is independent of any "},{"type":"text","text":"SessionId","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":"."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A current "},{"type":"text","text":"health state","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" that MAY change over the entity's lifetime."}]}]}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A Health Entity is not mutated by direct field assignment. Instead, its state changes are driven by "},{"type":"text","text":"health state-change messages","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" addressed to the entity's identifier. Each message declares an observed or requested state transition for that entity; the entity's current state is the result of applying these messages in order. This mirrors the message-driven model used for Steps: producers emit state-change messages, and the entity's state is derived from them rather than shared as mutable memory."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Health state-change messages MAY be "},{"type":"text","text":"consumed by a health agent (or health manager)","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" — a long-lived role, separate from the flow, that aggregates the state of many Health Entities, exposes a consolidated view, and MAY react to degraded or failed states (for example, by raising alarms or disabling a component). The health agent is a consumer of health state changes, not a Session Coordinator, and MUST NOT drive Session transitions."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The flow model is unrelated to Health Entities. A Step MAY emit a health state-change message or read a Health Entity's current state, but health observation is orthogonal to coordination and never drives Session transitions. The internal state model of a Health Entity, the vocabulary of its state-change messages, and how the health agent aggregates or polls them are out of scope for this RFC."}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"5.8 Session Initiation"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A Session does not exist until it is initiated. Initiation is the act of minting a new "},{"type":"text","text":"SessionId","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" for a given "},{"type":"text","text":"(ScriptId, ScriptVersion)","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" and recording the first event in a new Session Log, from which the first Step is activated."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Initiation is driven by a "},{"type":"text","text":"trigger","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" that is external to any running Session. A trigger is typically:"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"a user action (for example, a card inserted or a button pressed), or"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"a timer or scheduled event, or"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"another observable condition in the environment."}]}]}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The mechanism is a "},{"type":"text","text":"start signal and a poll","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":". A Session Script MAY declare a start signal; emitting that signal activates an initiation poll that evaluates the trigger condition and decides whether a new Session should begin:"}]},{"type":"orderedList","attrs":{"order":1},"content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A start signal is emitted (by a device, UI, scheduler, or other source)."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"An "},{"type":"text","text":"initiator","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" evaluates the trigger condition for the candidate "},{"type":"text","text":"(ScriptId, ScriptVersion)","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":"."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"If the condition holds, the initiator mints a new "},{"type":"text","text":"SessionId","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" and records the first event, activating the first Step."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"If the condition does not hold, no Session is created and no state is retained."}]}]}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The initiator is a distinct role from a Step: it runs before any Session exists and therefore cannot rely on "},{"type":"text","text":"SessionId","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" or Session data. It is a purely runtime role and is not part of the Session Script interface. Typically the initiator is the environment's "},{"type":"text","text":"idle loop","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" — the resting state that continuously watches for a trigger and is the natural home for the \"may a new Session begin?\" decision. Evaluation of the trigger condition MUST be idempotent with respect to a single start signal, so that a repeated or redelivered signal does not create duplicate Sessions for the same triggering event."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Note (non-normative): idempotency is commonly realized by edge detection on the trigger — for example debouncing a hardware input, read-and-clear consumption of a UI button press, or a one-shot flag — so that a level-held or redelivered signal initiates at most one Session."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The declaration of a Session's start signal and trigger binding is a Session Script concern (see SOPC-002)."}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"6. Session Log"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The Session Log is the authoritative append-only event history for a Session."}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"6.1 Required Properties"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Each event in the Session Log MUST include:"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A globally unique event identifier."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A session identifier."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A step identifier."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A timestamp."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A schema version."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A payload."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Correlation and causation identifiers where applicable."}]}]}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The Session Log MUST be append-only."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The Session Log MUST support replay to rebuild Session state deterministically."}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"6.2 Schema Evolution"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Event schemas MUST be versioned."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Schema changes SHOULD preserve backward compatibility for existing readers."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Breaking schema changes MUST be introduced with a new schema version and explicit migration strategy."}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"7. Session Data Model"}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"7.1 State Contract"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Session state MUST be represented as typed, immutable value objects."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The wire and at-rest encoding of Session data (a form keyed by globally unique data-element identifiers, per SOPC-002 §9) is an implementation detail and MUST NOT be the normative state contract. Adapters MAY translate between such encodings and the typed value objects, but the value objects remain authoritative."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A free-form dictionary MAY be used only as an internal adapter detail and MUST NOT be the normative Session state contract. Such a dictionary is a transitional adapter, for example when migrating a legacy shared structure; the target design is copy-by-value into declared data-elements (§7.5)."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Each Step MAY contribute additional value objects to Session state, but MUST NOT mutate prior events."}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"7.2 Local Persistence"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A Step MAY persist the Session data it produces locally, keyed by "},{"type":"text","text":"(SessionId, StepId)","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":"."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Where a Step persists data locally, the Session Log remains authoritative: local storage is a materialized projection and MUST be reconstructable by replaying the Session Log. Loss of a local store MUST NOT cause permanent loss of Session data."}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"7.3 Data Query"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A Step SHALL support a "},{"type":"text","text":"Query","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" (Section 5.4) that returns the global data object(s) it holds for a given "},{"type":"text","text":"(SessionId, StepId)","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":"."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Collecting the full data set for a Session is performed by querying each participating Step, or by querying a projection built from the Session Log. The result identifies each data object by its global data-element identifier (defined in the Session Script, see SOPC-002)."}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"7.4 Retention and Persistence Requirements"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Local Session data retention SHALL be bounded by an explicit policy. This RFC defines the requirement; concrete durations are deployment configuration."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The following are "},{"type":"text","text":"open for decision","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" and MUST be resolved before ratification:"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Minimum retention window for locally persisted Session data after a Session reaches a terminal state."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Whether retention is driven by time, by Session terminal state, or by explicit purge command."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Durability requirement for local data (in-memory, crash-durable, or replicated) relative to the authority of the Session Log."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Behavior of a "},{"type":"text","text":"Query","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" against data that has been purged locally but is still derivable from the Session Log."}]}]}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"7.5 External Entities and Copy-by-Value"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Session data MUST NOT hold a live reference or pointer to an external entity (for example, a customer, agreement, or device record) that is owned and mutated outside the Session."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Instead, a Step that needs data from an external entity SHALL resolve that entity during its execution and "},{"type":"text","text":"copy the needed fields as values into Session data","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":", published as the Step's output data-elements. From that point the Session carries a self-contained snapshot: subsequent Steps consume the copied values, not the external source."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"This preserves the immutability contract of §7.1 and the replay guarantee of §6: because the snapshot is captured in a "},{"type":"text","text":"Done","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" event at the moment of resolution, replaying the Session Log reproduces the same values regardless of later changes to the external entity. A Step that requires a fresher value MUST re-resolve the entity and publish a new data-element, rather than aliasing the original source."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Until resolved, a Participant SHOULD retain local Session data at least until the Session reaches a terminal state and all compensations have completed."}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"8. Query and Derivation"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The platform MUST support querying by "},{"type":"text","text":"SessionId","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":"."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The platform MUST support querying by Step within a Session."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The platform MUST support querying a Step for its global data objects (Section 7.3)."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The platform SHOULD support filtering by event type and time range."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Derived business artifacts (for example, receipt or ticket views) MAY be produced from projections over the Session Log."}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"9. Reliability and Failure Handling"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Delivery semantics depend on the deployment profile (Section 10). Where delivery is not exactly-once, delivery MAY be at-least-once and Consumers MUST deduplicate by event identifier. In a single-process profile where delivery is exactly-once, deduplication MAY be a no-op."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Retries SHOULD use bounded backoff policies."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"If a Step cannot complete after retries, the Participant MUST emit a "},{"type":"text","text":"Failed","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" event and include failure metadata sufficient for compensation or operator handling."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"If a "},{"type":"text","text":"Rollback","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" cannot complete after retries, the Participant MUST emit a "},{"type":"text","text":"Failed","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" event for the compensation and escalate for operator handling."}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"10. Deployment Profiles"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"SOPC defines a single abstraction — one Step contract, one Session Script — that is realized across a spectrum of deployments. The abstraction is invariant; the mechanisms that realize it differ by profile. Conformance to SOPC does "},{"type":"text","text":"not","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" require a message broker or a distributed infrastructure."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Two reference profiles anchor the spectrum:"}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"10.1 Single-Process Profile"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"One process runs the flow, typically one Session at a time (for example, a payment terminal)."}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The four Step messages ("},{"type":"text","text":"Activation","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":", "},{"type":"text","text":"Rollback","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":", "},{"type":"text","text":"Done","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":", "},{"type":"text","text":"Failed","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":") and "},{"type":"text","text":"Query","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" MAY be realized as in-process calls or an in-memory queue rather than broker messages."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Delivery is effectively exactly-once; deduplication (Section 9) MAY be a no-op."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The Session Coordinator (SOPC-002 §7.1) is centralized and generic."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"ScriptId","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":"/"},{"type":"text","text":"ScriptVersion","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" checks (SOPC-002 §6) are trivially satisfied by the single deployment, but still apply."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The Session Log (Section 6) remains valuable for crash recovery, for example after a power cycle."}]}]}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A single-process realization is a first-class, conformant SOPC deployment. Features intended for distribution are latent — present in the model but trivially satisfied — and MUST NOT be treated as prerequisites."}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"10.2 Distributed Profile"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Steps are hosted by independent services, potentially running many concurrent Sessions."}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The Step messages are real messages over a broker; delivery is typically at-least-once, so deduplication by event identifier is mandatory (Section 9)."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Compensation ("},{"type":"text","text":"Rollback","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":") is real distributed-Saga behaviour."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The Session Coordinator MAY be distributed (Participants self-activate) or a centralized generic driver (SOPC-002 §7.1)."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Concurrent Sessions make "},{"type":"text","text":"SessionId","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" partitioning and ordered delivery live concerns."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Copy-by-value (Section 7.5) is a necessity, since a live reference across a service boundary is not possible."}]}]}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The additional coordination challenges of the distributed profile — notably ownership of the Session Log across service boundaries — are the subject of ongoing work (Section 13)."}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"10.3 Profile Invariance"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The Step contract, the Session Script, the Session Log semantics, and copy-by-value are identical across profiles. Moving a flow from single-process to distributed changes the realization of message delivery and the placement of the Session Coordinator, but MUST NOT change the Session Script or the Step contracts."}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"10.4 Heterogeneous-Language Boundary (Non-Normative)"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A deployment commonly spans "},{"type":"text","text":"several implementation languages","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" at once — for example C# microservices, C++ embedded firmware, and Java / Kotlin on Android — that must all coordinate within the same flow. Wherever a Step message ("},{"type":"text","text":"Activation","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":", "},{"type":"text","text":"Rollback","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":", "},{"type":"text","text":"Done","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":", "},{"type":"text","text":"Failed","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":", "},{"type":"text","text":"Query","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":") crosses from one language runtime to another, it is realized as a serialized message (for example JSON over a broker) rather than an in-process call."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Such a boundary is a "},{"type":"text","text":"transport-adapter concern only","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" and is deliberately not modeled by SOPC. Each language runtime hosts a translational endpoint that serializes and deserializes Step messages and maps them field-by-field onto its local contract ? it contains no coordination or business logic. Existing repository examples demonstrate this shape on two sides of the boundary already: a C# consumer ("},{"type":"text","text":"docs/ap/examples/AP-003-MassTransit-Consumer-CSharp.md","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":") and its C++ equivalent with a purely translational "},{"type":"text","text":"MessageMapping","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" ("},{"type":"text","text":"docs/ap/examples/AP-003-RabbitMQ-Consumer-Cpp.md","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":"); a Java / Kotlin (Android) endpoint is a further adapter of the same shape."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The interoperability surface between languages is therefore the "},{"type":"text","text":"message schema, not shared code or shared types","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":". Because each runtime owns its own local contract behind a translational adapter, adding a language (for example Kotlin on Android) adds one more adapter over the same message vocabulary and requires no change to any other Participant."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Two consequences follow from the model already defined above:"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Because the boundary only serializes the five Step messages, it introduces no new Step, Session, or Session Log semantics. A Participant hosting a Step handler is an ordinary Participant (Section 5.6) regardless of whether it is written in C#, C++, Java, or Kotlin; the language is invisible to the Session Script."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Copy-by-value (Section 7.5) is already mandatory whenever a message crosses a process boundary, so a live cross-language reference is never required; the snapshot carried on the message is sufficient for the receiving runtime to act."}]}]}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A deployment that bridges, say, an in-process C# coordinator to local C++ device handlers and to an Android (Kotlin) UI remains a conformant Single-Process (Section 10.1) or Distributed (Section 10.2) realization depending on whether the bridged messages are in-memory or broker-carried. The number of languages involved adds no requirement to this RFC."}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"11. Security and Compliance"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Sensitive payload fields MUST be classified and protected at rest and in transit."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Access to Session, Session Log, and locally persisted Session data MUST be scoped by least privilege."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Redaction rules SHOULD be defined for derived views used outside trusted operational boundaries."}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"12. Relationship to Sagas"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A Saga is a specialization of SOPC where the flow concerns distributed business transactions."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"SOPC generalizes this model to any stateful interaction, including UI wizards, terminal flows, provisioning workflows, and device update pipelines."}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"13. Open Questions"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Genuine spec-level questions:"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Durability class for locally persisted Session data (Section 7.4), in particular whether a power-cycling terminal requires crash-durable local persistence."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Staleness policy for copied external-entity snapshots (Section 7.5): when a Step MUST re-resolve rather than reuse a prior copy."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Ownership of the Session Log across service boundaries in the distributed profile (Section 10.2)."}]}]}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Deferred to implementation, not this RFC:"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Retention window durations for locally persisted Session data (deployment configuration, Section 7.4)."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Whether a "},{"type":"text","text":"Query","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" collects data by scatter-gather across Steps or from a central Session Log projection."}]}]}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"14. References"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"[1] S. Layering Rules"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The "},{"type":"text","text":"Core","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" — Application, Domain, Shared Kernel, and Capabilities — depends on nothing outside itself (AP-001 §3, AP-002 §4.1)."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Within the Core, references move inward: Application → Domain → Capabilities → Shared Kernel, with the Shared Kernel depending on nothing (AP-002 §7)."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Implementations (Host Units, Deployment Units—including Composition Units—and Adapter Implementations) MAY reference the Core; the Core MUST NOT reference any Implementation."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Each Host Unit MUST reference exactly one Core Unit (through that Core's Application layer)."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Deployment Units MAY compose multiple Host Units and MAY therefore deploy multiple Core Units in one process."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"No cyclic dependencies are permitted between Units at any layer."}]}]}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"6. Enforcement"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The dependency directions of §4 SHALL be enforced mechanically by the build system, expressed per language:"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"C#","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" — project references are restricted to permitted directions. The Core project declares no reference to any Host, Deployment/Composition, or Adapter project. Intra-Core folder directions (Application/, Domain/, Capabilities/, SharedKernel/) are enforced by namespace discipline and code review (AP-002 §7)."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"C++","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" — target dependencies and header visibility are restricted to permitted directions. The Core target declares no dependency on Host or Adapter targets; include-path discipline enforces intra-Core directions (AP-002 §7)."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Where available, an automated architecture test (for example, a fitness function asserting the matrix of §4) SHOULD be added so violations fail the build rather than relying solely on review."}]}]}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"7. Compliance"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A service complies with this AP if:"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Every reference between Units conforms to the direction permitted by the matrix in §4."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The Core (SharedKernel, Capabilities, Domain, Application) references no Host, Deployment, or Adapter Unit (§3, §5)."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The Shared Kernel references nothing (§4)."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Each Host Unit references exactly one Core Unit, and Host/Deployment Units reach business behaviour only through the Application, never by depending on the Domain or Capabilities directly (§4, §5)."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Deployment Units may compose multiple Host Units from one or many Core Units without introducing Core-to-Core dependencies."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Adapter Implementations depend on the Domain only through the Capability contracts they implement (§4)."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"No cyclic dependencies exist between Units (§5)."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The permitted directions are enforced by the build system (§6)."}]}]}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"8. References"}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"8.1 Normative References"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"[1] S. Introduction"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"This AP defines the physical structure of a service that realizes the logical architecture defined in AP-001 [3]."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"AP-001 defines architectural invariants — the logical areas (Application, Domain, Shared Kernel, and Capabilities) and the direction in which dependencies must flow. It deliberately says nothing about projects, folders, or assemblies."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"This AP supplies that missing layer. It specifies how the logical areas of AP-001 are organized into concrete units of compilation, how those units may reference one another, and how they are named. Where AP-001 asks "},{"type":"text","text":"what a service is","marks":[{"type":"em"}]},{"type":"text","text":", this AP answers "},{"type":"text","text":"how the core of a service is arranged","marks":[{"type":"em"}]},{"type":"text","text":"."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"This AP covers only the terms AP-001 introduces: the Application, the Domain, the Domain's Capabilities, and the generic notion of an Implementation. Examples use a single, language-neutral notation; casing and naming differences between languages are governed by the rule in Section 6.1 rather than by parallel examples."}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"1.1 Structure Does Not Imply Deployment"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The structure defined in this AP — separate Units for Domain, Application, and various Implementation concerns — describes "},{"type":"text","text":"logical boundaries","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":", not deployment topology."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Multiple separate Units MAY be:"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Deployed as a single process"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Deployed as independent processes"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Combined in any configuration that serves operational needs"}]}]}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The separation into Units ensures modularity, testability, and technology independence (AP-001 §4.1). It does not mandate distributed deployment. Deployment decisions are optimizations made when operational requirements justify the overhead."}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"2. Normative Language"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The key words "},{"type":"text","text":"MUST","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":", "},{"type":"text","text":"MUST NOT","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":", "},{"type":"text","text":"SHALL","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":", "},{"type":"text","text":"SHOULD","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":", and "},{"type":"text","text":"MAY","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" in this document are to be interpreted as described in BCP 14 (RFC 2119 [1] and RFC 8174 [2]) when, and only when, they appear in all capitals, as shown here."}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"3. Core is divided into exactly four peer folders. This section is informative; it records why the split is drawn where it is."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The split follows a single organizing principle: "},{"type":"text","text":"each folder isolates concerns that change for a different reason and that depend in a single direction.","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" AP-001 defines only three logical areas (Application, Domain, Capabilities). The four folders realize those three areas plus one supporting peer, chosen as follows."}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Domain/","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" holds the business rules — the reason the service exists. It is the innermost concern and depends on nothing outside the Core. It is a peer, not a parent, so that its rules are not entangled with orchestration or transport."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Application/","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" holds use cases and Contract Models — the orchestration of Domain rules for a specific purpose.
