It is promoted to a peer — rather than being placed inside any one folder — precisely because more than one folder needs it. Nesting it inside "},{"type":"text","text":"Domain/","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" would force "},{"type":"text","text":"Capabilities/","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" or "},{"type":"text","text":"Application/","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" to reach into the Domain for a value type, introducing the awkward or bidirectional dependencies that Section 5.1.2 exists to prevent."}]}]}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The four are peers rather than a nested hierarchy because their permitted dependencies flow in one direction (Section 7) without any folder containing another. A nested layout would imply ownership relationships that the dependency rules deliberately do not grant."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The count is deliberately small. Additional Core-level folders are not introduced for concerns that belong to Implementations (which live in Host Units, per AP-003) or to cross-cutting utilities (such as the pre-AP shared "},{"type":"text","text":"Core","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" folder documented in Background-LegacyFolderMigration). Keeping the Core to these four peers preserves the property that the Core is verifiable without any Implementation and without deployment (Section 8)."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"On naming: each folder is named for the concept it realizes in AP-001's ubiquitous language rather than for a technology or a layer number. Three of the four names are drawn directly from established literature and need no new justification:"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Domain/","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" realizes the "},{"type":"text","text":"Domain Model","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" [8] — the layer Evans defines as holding the business concepts, rules, and state that are the reason the software exists."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Application/","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" realizes the "},{"type":"text","text":"Application Layer","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" [8] — Evans's thin layer that directs work by coordinating the Domain Model, holding no business rules of its own."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"SharedKernel/","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" retains Evans's "},{"type":"text","text":"Shared Kernel","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" [8] name, the explicitly shared subset of the model that multiple parts agree to keep consistent, to avoid inventing a synonym for a well-understood concept."}]}]}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The fourth name, "},{"type":"text","text":"Capabilities/","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":", is the only term this AP does not inherit verbatim from Evans. Its conceptual grounding — as the driven (secondary) ports of Hexagonal Architecture applying the Dependency Inversion Principle — is established in AP-001 §6.5 [3] and is not restated here. This AP is concerned only with realizing that logical area as a peer folder. \"Capabilities\" is preferred over the bare word \"Ports\" because it names the folder for what the domain requires (the capabilities it needs from the outside world) in the ubiquitous language of the service, rather than for the structural metaphor; the two terms are synonymous for the purposes of this AP."}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"5. Reference Rules"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Unit references SHALL enforce the Dependency Rule of AP-001: dependencies move toward business meaning, and the Core depends upon nothing outside itself."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Within the Core Unit, the following internal reference directions are permitted:"}]},{"type":"codeBlock","attrs":{"language":"text"},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"Application/ ──▶ Domain/\nApplication/ ──▶ SharedKernel/\nApplication/ ──▶ Capabilities/\nDomain/ ──▶ SharedKernel/\nDomain/ ──▶ Capabilities/         (Domain may reference its own capability interfaces)\nCapabilities/ ──▶ SharedKernel/   (Capabilities may use shared business datatypes in their contracts)"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The following references are prohibited:"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"SharedKernel/","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" to "},{"type":"text","text":"Domain/","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":", "},{"type":"text","text":"Capabilities/","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":", or "},{"type":"text","text":"Application/","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":"."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Capabilities/","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" to "},{"type":"text","text":"Domain/","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" or "},{"type":"text","text":"Application/","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":"."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Domain/","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" to "},{"type":"text","text":"Application/","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" (within Core)."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Core Unit to any Host Unit."}]}]}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A Host Unit MUST reference exactly one Core Unit (through that Core's Application layer). The Core Unit MUST NOT reference any Host Unit."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Deployment Units MAY compose multiple Host Units and, by composition, may deploy functionality from multiple Core Units in one process."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"These rules SHALL be enforced by the build system, expressed as follows in each language."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"C#","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" — enforcement is by project reference and namespace organization. The Core is a single project. Folder-level dependencies within the Core (Domain/ vs SharedKernel/ vs Capabilities/ vs Application/) are enforced by namespace visibility and code review. The Core project declares no project references to Host projects. Each Host project SHOULD reference exactly one Core project; Deployment projects MAY reference multiple Host/Core projects as needed for composition."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"C++","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" — enforcement is by target dependency and header visibility. The Core is a single library target. Folder-level dependencies are enforced by include path discipline and header guards. The Core target declares no dependencies on Host targets. Each Host target SHOULD depend on exactly one Core target; Deployment targets MAY depend on multiple Host/Core targets as needed for composition."}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"8. Testing Structure"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The physical structure SHALL support the testability required by AP-001 [3]."}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The Core Unit (containing "},{"type":"text","text":"Domain/","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":", "},{"type":"text","text":"SharedKernel/","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":", "},{"type":"text","text":"Capabilities/","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":", and "},{"type":"text","text":"Application/","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" folders) SHALL be verifiable by tests that reference only the Core Unit, without any Implementation and without deployment."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Test Units SHALL follow the same reference rules: tests MAY reference the Core Unit and exercise its public API, but the Core MUST NOT reference test Units."}]}]}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"As a suggested organization, test projects SHOULD follow the same Unit structure as production code and be separated by test category. For example:"}]},{"type":"codeBlock","attrs":{"language":"text"},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"MyPaymentService.Core/\nMyPaymentService.Core.UnitTests/\n\nMyPaymentService.Infrastructure.Sql/\nMyPaymentService.Infrastructure.Sql.IntegrationTests/\n\nMyPaymentService.HttpHost/\nMyPaymentService.HttpHost.IntegrationTests/\n\nMyPaymentService.EndToEndTests/"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"This suggestion is intended to preserve traceability between production Units and their tests while keeping test categories explicit. Unit tests typically align to Core Units, integration tests to technology-specific Units, and end-to-end tests to the fully composed service."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"This structure is consistent with the test pyramid [7]."}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"9. Scope and Evolution"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"This AP defines the structure of the Core: a single Core Unit organized into four peer folders ("},{"type":"text","text":"Domain/","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":", "},{"type":"text","text":"SharedKernel/","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":", "},{"type":"text","text":"Capabilities/","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":", and "},{"type":"text","text":"Application/","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":")."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Host Units (incoming implementations) are defined in AP-003. The internal structure of transport-specific endpoints, mappings, how Hosts organize composition, and how configuration is managed are all specified in AP-003."}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"10. Compliance"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A service complies with this AP i < args.Length; i++)\n        {\n            string arg = args[i];\n\n            if (arg.StartsWith(\"--\"))\n            {\n                string optionName = arg.Substring(2);\n\n                // Check if next argument is a value (doesn't start with --)\n                if (i + 1 < args.Length && !args[i + 1].StartsWith(\"--\"))\n                {\n                    _options[optionName] = args[i + 1];\n                    i++; // Skip the value\n                }\n                else\n                {\n                    // It's a flag\n                    _flags.Add(optionName);\n                }\n            }\n            else\n            {\n                _positionalArgs.Add(arg);\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    public string GetCommand() => _command;\n\n    public bool HasFlag(string flag) => _flags.Contains(flag);\n\n    public string GetOption(string option) =>\n        _options.TryGetValue(option, out var value) ? value : string.Empty;\n\n    public string GetPositional(int index) =>\n        index < _positionalArgs.Count ? _positionalArgs[index] : string.Empty;\n\n    public bool IsValid() => !string.IsNullOrEmpty(_command);\n}"}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"4. Deployment Composition Configuration"}]},{"type":"codeBlock","attrs":{"language":"csharp"},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"// Commerce.Api/Program.cs\nusing MyPaymentService.Core.Application.UseCases;\nusing MyPaymentService.Core.Domain.Services;\nusing MyPaymentService.Core.Capabilities.Persistence;\nusing MyPaymentService.Core.Capabilities.ExternalServices;\nusing MyPaymentService.Core.SharedKernel.Money;\n\nvar builder = WebApplication.CreateBuilder(args);\n\n// Add controllers\nbuilder.Services.AddControllers();\nbuilder.Services.AddEndpointsApiExplorer();\nbuilder.Services.AddSwaggerGen();\n\n// Domain configuration - injected as parameters\nvar maximumPaymentAmount = new Money(\n    builder.Configuration.GetValue<decimal>(\"Payment:MaximumAmount\"),\n    builder.Configuration.GetValue<string>(\"Payment:Currency\") ?? \"USD\");\n\nbuilder.Services.AddSingleton(maximumPaymentAmount);\n\n// Register domain services\nbuilder.Services.AddScoped<PaymentAuthorizationService>();\n\n// Register use cases\nbuilder.Services.AddScoped<AuthorizePaymentUseCase>();\nbuilder.Services.AddScoped<GetPaymentUseCase>();\n\n// Register capability implementations (adapters)\nbuilder.Services.AddScoped<IPaymentRepository, EntityFrameworkPaymentRepository>();\nbuilder.Services.AddScoped<IFraudDetectionService, ThirdPartyFraudDetectionService>();\n\nvar app = builder.Build();\n\n// Configure HTTP pipeline\nif (app.Environment.IsDevelopment())\n{\n    app.UseSwagger();\n    app.UseSwaggerUI();\n}\n\napp.UseHttpsRedirection();\napp.UseAuthorization();\napp.MapControllers();\n\napp.Run();"}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"6. MassTransit details stay in the adapter layer"}]}]}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The pattern is simple: "},{"type":"text","text":"receive message -> map to contract -> invoke use case -> publish event -> end","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":"."}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"See Also:","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"C# Implementation (AP-002)","marks":[{"type":"link","attrs":{"href":"#"}}]},{"type":"text","text":" - Base implementation"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"HTTP Host Example - C#","marks":[{"type":"link","attrs":{"href":"#"}}]},{"type":"text","text":" - Synchronous incoming implementation"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"RabbitMQ Host Example - C++","marks":[{"type":"link","attrs":{"href":"#"}}]},{"type":"text","text":" - C++ equivalent"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Compilable Example README","marks":[{"type":"link","attrs":{"href":"#"}}]},{"type":"text","text":" - Introduction"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"This AP specifies "},{"type":"text","text":"Adapter Implementations","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" — the concrete realizations of Domain Capabilities (AP-005) using specific technologies."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"An Adapter's sole responsibility is "},{"type":"text","text":"translation between two scopes","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":":"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Domain scope","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":": Domain terms, Shared Kernel Value Objects, domain concepts"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Technology scope","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":": HTTP, SQL, message formats, external APIs"}]}]}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Given a Capability expressed in domain terms and a target technology, the translation is straightforward: map domain concepts to technology concepts. This AP defines the principles governing that translation."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Adapters reside outside the core, may reference the core, and MUST NOT be referenced by it (AP-002 §7, AP-006). They contain no business decisions (AP-001 §6.1) and are replaceable without changing the Domain or Application."}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"2. Normative Language"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The key words "},{"type":"text","text":"MUST","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":", "},{"type":"text","text":"MUST NOT","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":", "},{"type":"text","text":"SHALL","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":", "},{"type":"text","text":"SHOULD","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":", and "},{"type":"text","text":"MAY","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" in this document are to be interpreted as described in BCP 14 (RFC 2119 [1] and RFC 8174 [2]) when, and only when, they appear in all capitals, as shown here."}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"3. Terminology"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The logical term "},{"type":"text","text":"Capability","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" retains the meaning defined in AP-001 [3] and AP-005 [5]."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Adapter","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" — A concrete implementation of a Capability that translates between domain scope and technology scope."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Domain Scope","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" — Concepts, types, and operations expressed in domain terms (Shared Kernel Value Objects, domain identifiers, domain operations)."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Technology Scope","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" — Concepts, types, and operations specific to a technology (SQL types, HTTP requests, message payloads, API clients)."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Translation","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" — The bidirectional mapping between domain scope and technology scope."}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"4. Adapter Responsibility"}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"4.1 Single Responsibility"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"An Adapter's sole responsibility is "},{"type":"text","text":"translation","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":":"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Inbound","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":": Technology → Domain (e.g., SQL row → Shared Kernel Value Object)"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Outbound","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":": Domain → Technology (e.g., Shared Kernel Value Object → SQL parameters)"}]}]}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"4.2 No Business Logic"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"An Adapter MUST NOT:"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Make business decisions"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Contain business rules"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Validate business invariants (those belong in the Domain)"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Orchestrate operations (that belongs in Use Cases)"}]}]}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"An Adapter MAY:"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Validate technology constraints (e.g., SQL field length, HTTP status codes)"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Handle technology-specific errors (e.g., connection timeouts, serialization failures)"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Implement retry logic, circuit breakers, or other resilience patterns"}]}]}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"4.3 Obvious Translation"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Given:"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A Capability interface in domain terms"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A target technology"}]}]}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The translation SHOULD be "},{"type":"text","text":"1:1 and obvious","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":":"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Each domain operation maps to a technology operation"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Each domain parameter maps to a technology parameter"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The structure mirrors the Capability interface"}]}]}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Example","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":":"}]},{"type":"codeBlock","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Capability (Domain Scope):\n  PaymentRecord Save(Money amount, string merchantId, PaymentStatus status)\n\nAdapter (Technology Scope - SQL):\n  PaymentRecord Save(...) {\n      // Money → decimal amount, string currency\n      // merchantId → VARCHAR parameter\n      // PaymentStatus → INT parameter\n      // Execute INSERT\n      // Return PaymentRecord (with generated ID)\n  }"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The mapping is mechanical, not creative."}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"5. Language-Specific Considerations"}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"5.1 Translation Idioms"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The mechanical nature of translation means Adapters follow language language features (LINQ, async/await, records)"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Follow .NET conventions"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Technology concerns (connection strings, HTTP clients) initialized at composition root"}]}]}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"C++","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":":"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Use constructor"}]}]}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"General","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":":"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The Capability interface is language-neutral in concept"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The Adapter follows the target language's conventions"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Both implement the same logical initialization (connection strings, API keys, HTTP clients, database connections) happens:"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"At "},{"type":"text","text":"composition root","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" (C#, using DI container)"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"In "},{"type":"text","text":"adapter constructor","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" (C++, passed as parameters)"}]}]}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Technology initialization MUST be done as close to the Adapter implementation as possible","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" (same repository/folder). This ensures:"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Technology concerns remain localized to the Adapter"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Dependencies and configuration are co-located with their usage"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Changes to technology choices affect only the Adapter Unit"}]}]}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The Domain and Application remain unaware of these details."}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"6. Placement and Structure"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Adapters live outside the core in separate technology-specific Units (AP-002 §6.2)."}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"6.1 Organization"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Adapters MAY be organized:"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"By technology","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":": All persistence adapters in one Unit, all messaging adapters in another"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"By capability","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":": Each Capability's adapter in its own Unit"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"By context","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":": Related adapters grouped together"}]}]}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The choice depends on service complexity and team preference. The key constraint: "},{"type":"text","text":"Adapters MUST NOT be referenced by the core.","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"For .NET services, the per-technology organization required by §9 takes precedence: each default technology SHALL reside in its own per-technology Adapter Unit."}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"6.2 Examples"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"By technology","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":":"}]},{"type":"codeBlock","content":[{"type":"text","text":"MyPaymentService.Infrastructure.Sqlite/\n├── SqlitePaymentRepository.cs\n├── SqliteTransactionRepository.cs\nMyPaymentService.Infrastructure.RabbitMq/\n├── RabbitMqEventPublisher.cs"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"By capability","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":":"}]},{"type":"codeBlock","content":[{"type":"text","text":"MyPaymentService.Infrastructure.PaymentRepository/\n├── SqlitePaymentRepository.cs\nMyPaymentService.Infrastructure.EventPublisher/\n├── RabbitMqEventPublisher.cs"}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"7. Constraints"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"An Adapter:"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"MUST implement a Capability interface (AP-005)"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"MUST translate between domain scope and technology scope"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"MUST NOT contain business logic or make business decisions"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"MUST NOT be referenced by the core (Domain or Application)"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"MAY use technology-specific libraries and patterns"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"MAY implement resilience patterns (retry, circuit breaker, timeout)"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"SHOULD have an obvious 1:1 translation from Capability to technology"}]}]}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"8. Adapter Initialization and Schema Readiness"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Some Adapters realize a Capability over a "},{"type":"text","text":"stateful backing store whose structure is versioned","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" — most commonly a Persistence Adapter over a relational schema. Bringing that store to the version the Adapter expects (schema migration) is a "},{"type":"text","text":"Technology Scope","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" concern (§3): it operates on the technology, applies rules internal to the Adapter, and produces a store at the required version. It is "},{"type":"text","text":"not","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" an Incoming Implementation (AP-003) and "},{"type":"text","text":"not","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" a Use Case (AP-001 §6.1); no Message, Contract Model, or Domain type participates."}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"8.1 Migration Is an Adapter Concern"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Schema migration and equivalent backing-store initialization SHALL be owned by the Adapter that realizes the affected Capability."}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The migration routine takes the current backing store and a target version, applies rules that are "},{"type":"text","text":"internal to the Adapter","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":", and yields a store at the target version. This has the same shape as any other operation the Adapter performs against its technology."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The target version is "},{"type":"text","text":"Adapter configuration","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" (§5.2), not a Capability Contract parameter. It MUST NOT be surfaced through the Capability interface (AP-005) or any Message."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Migration rules are technology-scope mechanics, permitted by §4.2. They MUST NOT encode business decisions and MUST NOT leak into the Domain, the Application, or any Host Unit (AP-003)."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Migration MUST NOT change the Capability's meaning: the Capability interface the core depends on is identical before and after migration."}]}]}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"8.2 Readiness Precondition"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"An Adapter over a versioned backing store SHALL expose an "},{"type":"text","text":"initialization step","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" that ensures the store is at the required version, and this step SHALL run before the Adapter serves any other operation."}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Initialization SHALL be idempotent: running it against an already-current store completes successfully without change."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"No Capability operation on the Adapter SHALL execute before initialization has completed successfully. An implementation MUST treat operations issued before readiness as an error rather than acting against an unmigrated store."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"By default, failure to reach the required version SHALL be treated as a fatal startup condition: the Adapter MUST NOT report readiness, and the composing process (AP-003 §5.3) SHOULD fail to start rather than serve traffic against an unmigrated store."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"An Adapter MAY offer a degraded, explicitly read-only mode where operating against the existing (unmigrated) store is safe and intentional. Such a mode SHALL be opt-in configuration and MUST NOT be the default."}]}]}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"8.3 Relationship to Hosts and Composition"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The "},{"type":"text","text":"trigger","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" for initialization is host composition, not the Adapter itself."}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A Host Unit's "},{"type":"text","text":"Configuration/","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" wiring (AP-003 §5.3) invokes the Adapter's initialization step as part of building infrastructure; the migration behaviour itself resides in the Adapter, not in the host."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Where migration is deployed as a separate step (for example an init container or one-shot job), the deployment wrapper is a thin composition of the Adapter (AP-003 §5.7) that runs only its initialization step. Such a wrapper MUST NOT contain migration logic of its own; it composes the Adapter that owns it."}]}]}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A worked C# example of this integration is provided in "},{"type":"text","text":"AP-007-Migration-CSharp.md","marks":[{"type":"link","attrs":{"href":"#"}}]},{"type":"text","text":"."}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"9. Technology Selections (.NET)"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The following technology selections apply to services built on the .NET platform. They are "},{"type":"text","text":"default recommendations","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":", not mandates: a .NET service SHOULD use them, and MAY use an alternative where justified. They do not bind services built on other platforms (for example C++)."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Each selection realizes a Domain Capability (AP-005) and SHALL reside in its own per-technology Adapter Unit (AP-002 §6.2). A selection MUST NOT leak its technology into the core (AP-001 §6.3)."}]},{"type":"table","attrs":{"layout":"default"},"content":[{"type":"tableRow","content":[{"type":"tableHeader","attrs":{"colspan":1,"rowspan":1,"colwidth":[340]},"content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Capability"}]}]},{"type":"tableHeader","attrs":{"colspan":1,"rowspan":1,"colwidth":[340]},"content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Default Pinned to v8 (last Apache 2.0 release); see version note below."}]}]}]}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"9.1 Persistence — Entity Framework"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Entity Framework is the default .NET persistence technology."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"It SHALL be confined to a Persistence Adapter Unit; the core MUST NOT reference it."}]}]}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"9.2 Messaging — MassTransit"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"MassTransit is the default .NET messaging technology."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Services are pinned to "},{"type":"text","text":"MassTransit v8 — the last release published under the open-source Apache 2.0 license.","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" Later releases moved to a commercial license, so services remain on this version."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"It SHALL be confined to a Messaging Adapter Unit; the core MUST NOT reference it."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Adopting a commercially licensed version is out of scope for this AP and is tracked separately."}]}]}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"These selections are outside the scope of the architectural principles. They are implementation conventions specific to .NET services within the organization."}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"10. Compliance"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A service complies with this AP if:"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Each Adapter implements exactly one Capability"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Adapters contain only translation logic, no business decisions"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The core does not reference any Adapter"}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Translation between domain and technology scope is direct and obvious"}]}]}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"11. References"}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"10.1 Normative References"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"[1] S. Bradner, \"Key words for use in RFCs to Indicate Requirement Levels,\" BCP 14, RFC 2119, March 1997. "},{"type":"inlineCard","attrs":{"url":"https://www.rfc-editor.org/info/rfc2119"}}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"[2] B. 2026-08-05T12:16:50.7314771Z {"version":1,"type":"doc","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"AP:","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" 009"},{"type":"hardBreak"},{"type":"text","text":"Status:","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" Draft"},{"type":"hardBreak"},{"type":"text","text":"Version:","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" 0.1"},{"type":"hardBreak"},{"type":"text","text":"Category:","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" Structural"},{"type":"hardBreak"},{"type":"text","text":"Author:","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" SJS"},{"type":"hardBreak"},{"type":"text","text":"Created:","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" 2026-07-10"},{"type":"hardBreak"},{"type":"text","text":"Supersedes:","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" None"},{"type":"hardBreak"},{"type":"text","text":"Depends on:","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" AP-001, AP-002, AP-004, AP-005, AP-006"}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"1. Introduction"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"This AP specifies the "},{"type":"text","text":"query side","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" of a service: how information is read from the service's state and returned to callers without invoking Domain business behaviour."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The write side of the architecture is fully specified by AP-001 through AP-007: business decisions are made in the Domain (AP-001 §6.1), expressed through Aggregates and Domain Services (AP-004), and persisted through Capabilities (AP-005) realized by Adapters (AP-007). That path deliberately routes every change through an Aggregate Root to protect invariants."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Reading does not make business decisions and does not mutate state. Routing every read through an Aggregate Root imposes cost without benefit and can distort the Aggregate design to serve presentation needs. This AP introduces "},{"type":"text","text":"Read Models","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" and "},{"type":"text","text":"Queries","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" as the read-side counterpart, so that reads are expressed directly and the write-side Aggregates remain focused on invariants."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"This AP is language-neutral in its normative rules. C#-specific mechanics (for example, record types and projection idioms) are governed by the .NET implementation profile, not by this AP."}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"1.1 Nature of the Optimization"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Read Models and Queries are a "},{"type":"text","text":"read-path optimization","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":", not a new Domain concern. The write side could, in principle, answer every read by loading an Aggregate and mapping it; this AP declines to do so because it would force Aggregate shapes to serve presentation, load more state than a read needs, and route reads through invariant-protection machinery they do not require."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The optimization is applied "},{"type":"text","text":"around","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" the Domain, not "},{"type":"text","text":"within","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" it:"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Read Models and Queries reside in the "},{"type":"text","text":"Application","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" (§6.1, §7), not the Domain."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A Query "},{"type":"text","text":"bypasses","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" Domain business behaviour entirely — it invokes no Domain Service, Aggregate operation, or write Capability (§7)."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Projection to Read Models occurs in a read-side "},{"type":"text","text":"Adapter","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" (Technology scope, §8), without materializing an Aggregate."}]}]}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The Domain therefore remains untouched and continues to own every business decision (AP-001 §6.1). The read side borrows only the Domain's "},{"type":"text","text":"ubiquitous language","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" — Query Capabilities are named in domain terms (§8) and Read Models MAY reuse Shared Kernel Value Objects (§6) — without depending on Domain "},{"type":"text","text":"types or behaviour","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":". This is the sense in which the read side is "},{"type":"text","text":"near","marks":[{"type":"em"}]},{"type":"text","text":" the Domain without being "},{"type":"text","text":"at","marks":[{"type":"em"}]},{"type":"text","text":" it."}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"2. Normative Language"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The key words "},{"type":"text","text":"MUST","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":", "},{"type":"text","text":"MUST NOT","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":", "},{"type":"text","text":"SHALL","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":", "},{"type":"text","text":"SHOULD","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":", and "},{"type":"text","text":"MAY","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" in this document are to be interpreted as described in BCP 14 (RFC 2119 [1] and RFC 8174 [2]) when, and only when, they appear in all capitals, as shown here."}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"3. Terminology"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The logical terms "},{"type":"text","text":"Application","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":", "},{"type":"text","text":"Domain","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":", "},{"type":"text","text":"Aggregate","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":", "},{"type":"text","text":"Entity","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":", "},{"type":"text","text":"Value Object","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":", and "},{"type":"text","text":"Capability","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" retain the meanings defined in AP-001, AP-004, and AP-005."}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Read Model","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" — An immutable, data-carrying type shaped for a specific read purpose. A Read Model expresses information for presentation or integration, not business state that governs decisions."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Query","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" — An Application operation that returns a Read Model (or a collection of Read Models) without invoking Domain business behaviour and without mutating state."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Projection","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" — The mapping that produces a Read Model directly from the underlying store, without materializing a Domain Aggregate."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Query Capability","marks":[{"type":"strong"}]},{"type":"text","text":" — A Capability (AP-005) that expresses a read requirement in domain terms, realized by a read-side Adapter (AP-007)."}]}]}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"4. Scope"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"In scope:"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Definition and placement of Read Models."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Queries as read-only Application operations."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Projection of Read Models directly from the underlying store."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The dependency direction of the read side and its relationship to Capabilities and Adapters."}]}]}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Out of scope:"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Business decisions and state mutation (write side, AP-001 §6.1, AP-004)."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Concrete query technologies (SQL, search indices) — see AP-007."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Transport shaping of query responses — see AP-003."}]}]}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"5. Separation of Read and Write"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A Query MUST NOT make a business decision (AP-001 §6.1) and MUST NOT mutate state."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A Query MUST NOT return a Domain Aggregate, Entity, or mutable Domain type. It SHALL return a Read Model."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A write operation MUST continue to route through an Aggregate Root (AP-004 §6.3); the read side does not weaken that rule."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The read side and write side MAY share the same underlying store, or MAY use separate stores. This is a deployment and technology decision (AP-001 §4.1), not an architectural requirement of this AP."}]}]}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"6. Read Models"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A Read Model SHALL be immutable and data-carrying, defined by its values."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A Read Model SHALL be shaped for a specific read purpose rather than mirroring an Aggregate's internal structure."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A Read Model MAY reuse Shared Kernel Value Objects (AP-004 §7) where that preserves the ubiquitous language, but MUST NOT depend on Domain Aggregates or Entities."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A Read Model MUST remain a pure data type: it MUST NOT carry transport, persistence, or serialization concerns (AP-004 §7.3). Any such binding is applied by the relevant Implementation (AP-003, AP-007)."}]}]}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"6.1 Placement"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Read Models SHALL reside in the Application folder of the Core Unit (AP-002 §5.1.4), alongside the Queries that return them."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Read Models are Application concerns, not Domain concerns; they MUST NOT reside in the "},{"type":"text","text":"Domain/","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" folder."}]}]}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"7. Queries"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A Query SHALL be an Application operation that accepts primitive parameters or a request Contract Model (AP-003 §5) and returns one or more Read Models."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A Query SHALL obtain data through a Query Capability (§8) or a projection realized by a read-side Adapter; it MUST NOT embed technology-specific query logic in the Application."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A Query MUST NOT invoke a Domain Service, an Aggregate operation, or any write Capability."}]}]}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"8. Query Capabilities and Projection"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A read requirement SHALL be expressed as a Query Capability in the Core Unit's "},{"type":"text","text":"Capabilities/","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" folder (AP-005), stated in domain terms and independent of technology."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A Query Capability's contract SHALL return Read Models, not Domain Aggregates."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Projection from the underlying store to Read Models SHALL be performed by a read-side Adapter (AP-007). The Adapter maps store-specific shapes to Read Models directly, without materializing an Aggregate."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A read-side Adapter MUST NOT expose a technology-specific query abstraction (for example a live query object) beyond its own boundary; the query mechanics remain inside the Adapter (AP-005 §6.1, AP-006)."}]}]}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"9. Dependency Rules"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The read side obeys the dependency matrix of AP-006 §4 without exception:"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Read Models and Queries live in the Application and depend only on the Domain, Shared Kernel, and Capabilities."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Query Capabilities live in "},{"type":"text","text":"Capabilities/","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" and reference only the Shared Kernel."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Read-side Adapters live outside the Core, implement Query Capabilities, and MUST NOT be referenced by the Core."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The Core MUST NOT depend on any read-side Adapter."}]}]}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"10. Compliance"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"A service complies with this AP if:"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Every read is expressed as a Query that returns a Read Model and makes no business decision or mutation (§5, §7)."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Read Models are immutable, purpose-shaped, and free of Domain Aggregate/Entity dependencies and of transport/persistence concerns (§6)."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Read Models and Queries reside in the Application folder of the Core Unit (§6.1)."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Read requirements are expressed as Query Capabilities and realized by read-side Adapters through projection, without materializing Aggregates (§8)."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The read side conforms to the dependency matrix of AP-006 §4 (§9)."}]}]}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"11. Open Questions"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The following decisions are deferred to review before this AP is promoted beyond Draft:"}]},{"type":"bulletList","content":[{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Whether a dedicated read store (with synchronization from the write store) is permitted, recommended, or discouraged as a default (§5)."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Whether Query Capabilities are always required, or whether simple projections MAY be realized without a Capability abstraction for trivial reads (§8)."}]}]},{"type":"listItem","content":[{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The .NET default query technology and projection idiom, to be recorded as a technology selection (AP-007 §9) rather than in this language-neutral AP."}]}]}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":1},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"12. References"}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"12.1 Normative References"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"[1] S. Bradner, \"Key words for use in RFCs to Indicate Requirement Levels,\" BCP 14, RFC 2119, March 1997. "},{"type":"inlineCard","attrs":{"url":"https://www.rfc-editor.org/info/rfc2119"}}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"[2] B. "},{"type":"inlineCard","attrs":{"url":"https://www.rfc-editor.org/info/rfc8174"}}]},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"12.2 Informative References"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"[3] \"AP-001: Architectural Principles,\" this series."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"[4] \"AP-002: Service Structure,\" this series."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"[5] \"AP-004: Domain,\" this series."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"[6] \"AP-005: Domain Capabilities,\" this series."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"[7] \"AP-006: Dependency Rules,\" this series."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"[8] \"AP-007: Adapter Implementations,\" this series."}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"[9] M. The Consumer (Transport Endpoint)"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The consumer is the C++ equivalent of the C# "},{"type":"text","text":"IConsumer<AuthorizePaymentCommand>","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":". It receives a raw payload, maps it to an Application Contract, invokes the Use Case, and produces a serialized "},{"type":"text","text":"PaymentAuthorizedEvent","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" to publish. It holds a non-owning pointer to the Use Case and contains no business logic."}]},{"type":"codeBlock","attrs":{"language":"cpp"},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"// mypaymentservice_rabbitmq_host/consumers/authorize_payment_consumer.h\n#pragma once\n\n#include <string>\n\n#include \"mypaymentservice_core/application/use_cases/authorize_payment_use_case.h\"\n\nnamespace mypaymentservice::rabbitmq_host::consumers {\n\n/// @brief Transport endpoint (consumer) for payment authorization messages.\n/// @note This consumer contains NO business logic. All business decisions are\n///       made in the Domain layer, invoked via the Use Case (AP-003).\nclass AuthorizePaymentConsumer {\npublic:\n    explicit AuthorizePaymentConsumer(\n        core::application::use_cases::AuthorizePaymentUseCase* authorize_use_case);\n\n    /// @brief Handle a single delivered message.\n    /// @param message_payload Raw JSON payload delivered by the broker.\n    /// @return Serialized PaymentAuthorizedEvent JSON to be published.\n    std::string Consume(const std::string& message_payload);\n\nprivate:\n    core::application::use_cases::AuthorizePaymentUseCase* authorize_use_case_;  // Non-owning\n};\n\n} // namespace mypaymentservice::rabbitmq_host::consumers"}]},{"type":"codeBlock","attrs":{"language":"cpp"},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"// mypaymentservice_rabbitmq_host/consumers/authorize_payment_consumer.cpp\nstd::string AuthorizePaymentConsumer::Consume(const std::string& message_payload) {\n    // Deserialize transport message.\n    auto command_opt = mappings::MessageMapping::ParseAuthorizeCommand(message_payload);\n    if (!command_opt) {\n        // Invalid message: rethrow so the caller can dead-letter/retry.\n        throw std::runtime_error(\"Invalid AuthorizePaymentCommand payload\");\n    }\n    const auto& command = *command_opt;\n\n    // Map transport message -> Contract Model.\n    auto request = mappings::MessageMapping::ToContract(command);\n\n    // Execute use case (synchronous). All business decisions happen here.\n    auto result = authorize_use_case_->Execute(request);\n\n    // Map result -> outgoing event and serialize for publishing.\n    auto event = mappings::MessageMapping::ToEvent(result, command.correlation_id);\n    return mappings::MessageMapping::SerializeEvent(event);\n}"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The consumer deliberately rethrows on failure so the broker layer can nack/dead-letter the message, mirroring the C# consumer rethrowing so MassTransit can retry."}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"5. Composition Root"}]},{"type":"paragraph","content":[{"type":"text","text":"commerce_worker/main.cpp","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":" wires the Core (via the "},{"type":"text","text":"ServiceContainer","marks":[{"type":"code"}]},{"type":"text","text":"), invokes host configuration, loads broker configuration, and starts the runtime. The Core dependencies are identical to the CLI and HTTP infrastructure implementation units."}]},{"type":"codeBlock","attrs":{"language":"cpp"},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"// Create the transport endpoint (consumer).\nrabbitmq_host::consumers::AuthorizePaymentConsumer consumer(&authorize_use_case);\n\n// Load broker configuration (mechanical wiring) and start the host.\nauto config = rabbitmq_host::configuration::RabbitMqConfiguration::FromEnvironment();\nrabbitmq_host::configuration::RabbitMqHost host(config, &consumer);\n\nif (!host.Run()) {\n    std::cerr << \"Failed to start RabbitMQ host\\n\";\n    return 1;\n}"}]},{"type":"rule"},{"type":"heading","attrs":{"level":2},"content":[{"type":"text","text":"6.
